## Milestone 34 — Building-Block Program: Design and Spikes

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-25).** User: "push it and go with your recommendations for M34"; the program is approved with all eight recommendations. The program's approval gate, the counterpart of `tre`'s M93. Nothing is rebuilt until the design and decisions are agreed.

**Decisions for the user, each with a recommendation:**
- P1 **The YAML view format.** Recommended: **keep Tesserae's current schema unchanged for app authors** (`kind: Rect`/`Container`/`Text`/`Icon`, `style: {background, foreground, ...}`, `bindings:`, `handlers:`, `component:`/`with:`/`repeat:`, `include:`) and translate it to `tre`'s primitives inside Tesserae. Every existing view keeps working. The alternative, adopting `tre`'s new names (`box`, `fill`) in YAML, breaks every app's views for no user benefit.
- P2 **MD3 color science** (HCT, tonal palettes, dynamic schemes from a seed). Recommended: **depend on a maintained Python port of Google's `material-color-utilities`** (such as `materialyoucolor`), accepted only if an M34 spike shows its schemes match `tre`'s own `DynamicTheme` on a set of reference seeds, light and dark. Alternatives: port `tre`'s handed-over code to Python (D says it hands over its color science, scales and icon data), or keep a small Rust extension in Tesserae.
- P3 **Reactivity semantics.** Recommended: **reproduce `tre`'s behavior exactly**: same-value writes don't notify; `Computed` recomputes when a dependency changes and notifies only if its value changed (corrected 2026-09-25 from "lazy and glitch-free" after reading `tre`'s code: it's eager); `batch` runs each subscriber once; `untrack`; the re-entrant-write error. Proven by one behavioral test suite run against both `tre`'s classes and Tesserae's while 0.3.4 still has `tre`'s.
- P4 **Binding expressions.** Recommended: **port `binding.rs`'s safe, non-`eval` whitelist grammar exactly**, so every existing `{{ }}` expression means the same thing. While 0.3.4 has both, check the two evaluators agree on every expression in the repo.
- P5 **Migration order.** Recommended: **incremental, bottom-up, suite green after every step** (the ordering above). The alternative, a parallel rewrite switched over at the end, delays all feedback to the end.
- P6 **Public API.** Recommended: Tesserae **keeps every public name it exports today** (`Signal`, `ViewModel`, `App`, `instantiate`, `Repeater`, `load_view`, `tesserae.widgets.*`), now backed by its own code. One known break: a `tesserae.widgets` factory returns a `tre.Node` today. A stateful control (a checkbox's `checked`, a slider's `value`) needs Tesserae-side state once `tre` stops holding it. Recommended: return a small Tesserae widget object with `.node` and its state, and write down the break.
- P7 **Catalog scope.** Recommended: **rebuild the whole catalog**, all ~50 factories and 67 fragments, staged by category (M40–M42), with the node graph, docking presentation and app shell last. The alternative is to trim rarely-used widgets and name them as dropped.
- P8 **Which `tre` names to build on.** Recommended: **`tre` 0.3.4's new API only** (`window.create`, `set`/`get`/`animate`, `on`, `show_layer`, `fill`/`stroke_color`), which are the target names, so `tre` 0.3.5 is mostly deletions for Tesserae. Anything R1-renamed in 0.3.5 gets a short follow-up.

### Phase 1 — Design ✅
- Step 1: `docs/design/building-blocks.md` (new MkDocs "Design" section): what app authors see (nothing changes but the one P6 break), the layers, and the plan for reactivity, bindings, the spec compiler with its YAML → `tre` mapping table, the cascade rules, the reconciler, components, screens, theme and widgets, the rules (new API only, incremental, proved against `tre`, the gate check) and the measurements. Sized from `tre`'s source (non-test lines): binding evaluator ~530, cascade ~230, reconciler ~420, spec model ~530, builder ~1,080, theme spec ~140, MD3 color/typography/shape ~600, icons ~120. **Finding:** `tre`'s reactivity is already pure Python (`python/tre/__init__.py`, ~400 lines); only its dependency-recording stack is native (`_record_read`, `_begin_recording`, `_end_recording`), so M35 is mostly taking that module over. `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅
- Step 2: decisions P1–P8 settled: the user took all eight recommendations (P3's description corrected: `Computed` is eager, not lazy) — ✅
- Step 3: sent to the `tre` session at the user's request ("send the message to tre", 2026-09-25). It asks for M97 Phase 2 Step 1's migration guide, most useful as (a) a node-by-node mapping of each removed MD3 kind and factory (structure, properties, animation timings and curves, a11y role and state), (b) the form of the `engine-md3` handover, especially the icon data, and (c) any binding or cascade behavior that differs from its doc comments. It proposes the gate check: `tre` provides a switch or test shim that makes every M98–M99-removed Python name raise, naming its replacement, and Tesserae runs its full suite and examples with it on, then keeps that run in CI from M43. It asks whether target-api.md's migration table is the source of truth, and how the node graph should be built (the table says "framework", nothing says which primitive).   **`tre` replied (2026-09-25), agreeing, and started the work as its M97 Phase 2 (local commits; it pushes only with the user's go-ahead).** Gate check: a Python shim `tre/_removed.py`, switched on by `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED=1`, read in `tre/__init__.py`, so Tesserae's suite and examples run unmodified. It covers **all of 0.3.5**, M100's renames as well as M98–M99's deletions. Removed methods and classes raise `AttributeError` naming the replacement, and value-level renames (`animate("background")`, legacy `get` names) raise too. `_removed.py` is the machine-readable source of truth; a `tre` test keeps it in step with target-api.md's table. The gate passes when Tesserae's suite and examples are green with it on, and Tesserae keeps that run in CI from M43. Migration guide: a `tools/dump_widget.py` that dumps any widget's subtree as diffable JSON through the new `get()`, a reference page of all 57 legacy factories at their defaults, write-ups of behavior a dump can't show (state layer and ripple timings, state-change animations, how the Rust-painted kinds draw), and an audit of `binding.rs`/`cascade.rs` against their doc comments. Icons: `engine-md3` has 12 curated Material Symbols as verbatim SVG `d=` strings sharing view box `0 -960 960 960`, usable directly as `create("path", data=d, view_box=(0, -960, 960, 960))`; the color science, type, shape and motion scales come with exact values. Node graph: primitives — a clipped viewport box panned and zoomed by `translate_x`/`translate_y`/`scale`, nodes placed by `x`/`y` and dragged with `capture_pointer`, and edges on `canvas` (one per edge with `set_hit_test_path` for stroke-accurate hits, or one shared canvas with Tesserae's own nearest-edge maths). `tre` offered stroke-accurate hit-testing for `path` nodes as a later addition if Tesserae prefers paths. tre#12 closes with `View` in M98 unless the user wants a 0.3.x fix — ✅

### Phase 2 — Spikes ✅
- Step 1: `tools/spikes/cascade_cost.py`, a prototype of Tesserae's own pipeline (4-tier cascade, role colours, an `ast`-whitelisted binding evaluator with a Python recording stack) on `create`/`set`, against `tre`'s `View` on the same view: 2,000 boxes + 2,000 texts, a 4-rule stylesheet (baseline, kind, class, id), 500 texts bound to one `Signal`, median of 5. **Build 20.7 ms vs 11.0 ms; theme switch 2.8 vs 3.1 ms; one `Signal.set` reaching 500 nodes 0.7 vs 0.5 ms.** Both sides produce the same text, fills and corner radii. A profile puts half the build in the naive cascade, which scans every rule for every node, so M37 indexes rules by kind, class and id. Build is a one-off cost at load, and live updates are on par with `tre` — ✅
- Step 2: **P2 accepted.** Downloaded `materialyoucolor` 3.0.4 at the user's request ("download it"; PyPI, MIT, pure Python, depends only on Pillow) into a scratch venv, not `.venv`. Compared all 49 roles `tre`'s `ColorScheme.role` answers against `SchemeTonalSpot` + `MaterialDynamicColors`, for 8 seeds (including black and white), light and dark: 784 values. Its default `spec_version="2025"` (MD3 Expressive) differs in 695. With **`spec_version="2021"`**, which `tre`'s `material-colors` 0.4.2 follows, 752 match exactly. The other 32 are the four `on_*_container` roles in light mode: `tre` uses tone 10, current `material-color-utilities` tone 30. Taking **tone 10** from the scheme's own palettes for those four matches all 32. **784 of 784 exact.** So M38 uses materialyoucolor with `spec_version="2021"` and those four light roles at tone 10; moving to the newer colors is a separate, visible choice for later — ✅
- Step 3: `tools/spikes/checkbox_on_primitives.py`: an MD3 checkbox from `tre` 0.3.4's building blocks only, in ~70 lines. A 40×40 focusable target with `role="checkbox"`, `label` and `checked`; an 18×18 outlined box; a check `path` drawn in by animating `trim_end`; a clipped 40×40 state layer (hover 8%, keyboard focus 10%) with a ripple scaled from the press point; `focus_visible` so pointer focus shows no ring. **12/12 headless checks pass** with `simulate`/`advance`. Findings for M39/M40: a simulated `pointer_down` + `pointer_up` on a node is itself a click and focuses the node (without `focus_visible`); Space and Enter deliver `click`. Not checked: pixels (no Python read-back) and timings against the legacy kind, which wait on `tre`'s dump tool and behaviour write-ups — ✅

## Milestone 35 — Reactivity in Tesserae

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-25).** User: "push it and start M35". `tre` D5: `Signal`, `Computed`, `Effect`, `ViewModel`, `batch`, `untrack` move to Tesserae. Smaller than first scoped: `tre`'s implementation is already pure Python (~400 lines) apart from a native dependency-recording stack, which Tesserae replaces with its own.

### Phase 1 — Core ✅
- Step 1: `src/tesserae/reactive.py`, taken over from `tre`'s `python/tre/__init__.py` at v0.3.4 (MIT, same author) with the same behaviour (P3): unchanged writes don't notify; `Computed` recomputes eagerly and notifies only on a changed value; `batch` dedupes per callback across every pending signal and flushes at the outermost level, even when `fn` raises; `untrack`; the re-entrant-write `RuntimeError`; the same `_subscribe`/`_unsubscribe` duck-typing, so `Repeater` and bindings are unchanged. The recording stack is a Python list of frames — ✅
- Step 2: `tests/test_reactive.py`: 13 behavioural tests, each run against both `tre`'s implementation and Tesserae's (26 cases), all passing on both. Mutation-checked on Tesserae's side: removing the same-value skip, the batch dedupe or the re-entrancy guard each fails a test — ✅

### Phase 2 — Switch Over ✅
- Step 1: `tesserae.Signal`/`Computed`/`Effect`/`ViewModel`/`batch`/`untrack` are now Tesserae's (`View`/`Component` are still `tre`'s until M37). **The bridge until M36:** `tre` still evaluates `{{ }}` bindings in `View._attach` on its native recording stack, so a Tesserae read with no Tesserae frame open is passed on to `tre._core._record_read`, and a Tesserae frame (`Computed`, `Effect`, `untrack`) shadows `tre`'s, as an inner frame does on `tre`'s single stack. Tested: `tre` bindings follow Tesserae `Signal`s and `Computed`s, and an `untrack` inside a ViewModel method a binding calls hides its read from the binding. Removing the bridge, or the shadowing, each fails a test. The one case it can't mirror — a `tre` binding evaluated inside a Tesserae frame — is documented in the module; the bridge goes in M36. Finding for M36: `tre`'s grammar allows a zero-argument call only as a method on a named value (`info.described()`), not a bare `described()` — ✅
- Step 2: full suite on `tre` 0.3.4: **455 passed** (426 + 29), all 3 examples clean. Found and fixed on the way: an intermittent failure in M44's `test_a_broken_edit_is_logged_and_the_watcher_carries_on`, which checked for the DEBUG traceback line before the watcher thread had logged it (it now waits for it); 15 repeated runs clean — ✅
- Step 3: docs: `guide/reactivity.md` (whose code it is now, and a "Details worth knowing" section: unchanged writes, eager `Computed`, the re-entrancy error, Tesserae's classes not tracking `tre`'s, the binding bridge), `index.md`, `api/index.md`, `architecture.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`, the `multi_screen` example; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

---

## Milestone 36 — Bindings and Handlers in Tesserae

**Status: ✅ Complete — the evaluator; wiring moved to M37 (2026-09-25).** User: "push it and start M36"; Phase 2's staging chosen by the user: "Move wiring into M37". `binding.rs`'s evaluator moves to Tesserae (`tre` M98).

### Phase 1 — Evaluator ✅
- Step 1: `src/tesserae/binding.py`, a port of `tre`'s `engine-spec/src/binding.rs` (lexer, recursive-descent parser, evaluator) and `engine-py/src/binding.rs` (the Python resolver) at v0.3.4, quirks included (P4): pyo3's value extraction order (bool, an `i64` or anything with `__index__`, anything `float()` takes, str; else an opaque handle), the primitive operator table (same-type `-`/`*`/ordering, mixed `+`, `int / int` a float, int division by zero an error, float `inf`/`nan`, `==` false across types, 64-bit wrapping), Python operators for handles, short-circuit `and`/`or` returning an operand, and `tre`'s error text including Rust's `Debug` forms (`Some(Minus)`, `Str("a")`, `Float(1e21)`, per-evaluation `Handle(n)` numbering). Reads record on `tesserae.reactive`'s stack — ✅
- Step 2: `tests/test_binding_parity.py`: each expression evaluated by `tre` (through a real `View`, a Text node bound to it: a string result is the node's text, any other value appears in `tre`'s "expects a string binding, got …" error, an evaluation error by its message) and by Tesserae, compared exactly. 131 designed expressions covering every grammar rule, operator rule, handle case and parse error, plus every `{{ }}` expression used in the repo (35): **166/166 identical** (81 values, 82 errors, on `tre`'s side). Mutation-checked: `==` across types, 64-bit wrapping, the `i64` range check, int division by zero, `and` returning an operand, and Rust's float formatting each fail tests. 455 → 623 passed. Docs: new `guide/bindings.md` (what an expression can contain, the rules that differ from Python, errors, who evaluates it), linked from the reactivity guide; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 2 — Wiring: Moved to M37 ✅
- Step 1: probed `tre` 0.3.4's new API on nodes a `tre` `View` builds. `set(text=...)` on Text and TextField, and `fill` and `width`, all work, and a programmatic `set` fires no change event, while typing fires `input` then `change` — the semantics `tre` issue #12 asks for. But on the legacy stateful kinds it can't drive the widget: `set(checked=True)` on a Checkbox and `set(selected=True)` on a Switch set only the accessibility flag (`get_checked()` stays `False`), and neither `simulate("click")` nor the legacy `window.click` toggles one (a known limit, noted in `examples/todo_list/app.py`); only the legacy `set_checked`/`set_selected` reach that state — ✅
- Step 2: decision (user: "Move wiring into M37"): Tesserae wires bindings, `handlers:` and `two_way:` on the nodes it builds itself (M37), with the new API only (P8); bindings on `checked`/`selected`/`value` arrive with Tesserae's own controls (M40). Until M37, `tre`'s `View._attach` keeps evaluating bindings and M35's recording bridge stays. One binding system at a time, nothing written twice — ✅

## Milestone 37 — Declarative Engine on `tre` Primitives

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-25).** User: "push it and scope M37", then "push it and go with your recommendations for M37" — Q1–Q4 as recommended: tokens first (this milestone's Phase 1), the nine MD3 kinds through the legacy factories until M40, `tesserae.View` with today's surface plus `root`, and a tree differ for parity. Replaces `View`, `reconcile`, `instantiate`/`Component`, the style cascade, `tre`'s binding wiring (moved here from M36) and `Window.from_view`/`show_view`. The largest milestone in the program.

**Scoped against the source and by probes on `tre` 0.3.4 (2026-09-25):**
- **What `tre`'s builder does** (`engine-spec/src/build.rs`, ~1,080 non-test lines; `spec.rs` ~530): 15 kinds. Layout: a horizontal default, pixel lengths, uniform or per-side `padding`/`margin`, `gap`, `flex_grow`/`flex_shrink` (default 1)/`flex_basis`, `align_items`/`justify_content`. Paint: `background`/`foreground` as a theme role or a colour, `corner_radius` as a number or shape token, `opacity`, `border_*`, `elevation` as a number or MD3 level. Text: `typography_role` supplies family, weight, size and line height, otherwise `font_family` and `font_size` are required. Per-kind required fields with their own errors (a Rect needs `background`, a Text needs `foreground`); images; icons by name; and the state fields of the MD3 kinds (`checked`, `selected`, `value`, `hour`/`minute`).
- **It depends on the theme.** Roles, shape tokens, elevation levels and typography roles all resolve through `engine-md3`, so the builder needs M38's tokens first. **0.3.4 has no `elevation` property**, only `shadows` (checked: `set(elevation=3)` is an unknown property), so MD3's levels need Tesserae's own shadow table.
- **Five kinds map onto primitives:** `Rect`/`Container` to `box`, `Text` to `text`, `TextField` to `text_input`, `Image` to `image`; `Icon` becomes a `path` from `tre`'s 12 curated SVG `d=` strings; `Link` is a `text` plus `click`. **The nine MD3 kinds** (`Checkbox`, `RadioButton`, `Switch`, `Slider`, the three progress kinds, `LoadingIndicator`, `TimePickerDial`) have no primitive until M40. Probed: a node from a legacy window factory (`add_checkbox`) **can** be moved into a Tesserae-built tree on the same window and keeps working (`set_checked` still toggles it); a node from another window's tree can't.
- **Views need a window.** A 0.3.4 node belongs to one window's tree, while a `tre` `View` today is built without one. So Tesserae builds each screen in the app's window, and `load_view()` on its own builds in a private headless window.
- **Layout differences to handle:** 0.3.4's `window.root` has 16 px padding and a horizontal direction, while a `from_view` root has none, so `App` zeroes it. A `text` node has no intrinsic size in either system: a Text built by a `View` is 0 px tall without a height too, so there's no regression (content-sized text via `measure_text` is a later enhancement).
- **The suite's coupling:** 15 of 39 test files call `tre.View` directly, mostly the fragment tests comparing a `tre`-built fragment with `tesserae.widgets`; there are 135 `.node("…")` lookups, and 28 calls to the legacy `get_text()` plus a few `get_checked`/`set_checked` (removed in 0.3.5).

**Decisions for the user, each with a recommendation:**
- Q1 **Tokens first.** Recommended: **move M38's static tokens ahead, as this milestone's Phase 1.** That means colour roles via `materialyoucolor` (the settings M34 proved exact), the shape scale, elevation as MD3 key and ambient `shadows`, and the typography scale, with `tre`'s exact values. M38 keeps live re-theming, the OS light/dark switch and theme files. Alternative: read tokens from `tre` for now (`Window.theme`), which 0.3.5 removes.
- Q2 **The nine MD3 kinds until M40.** Recommended: **build them with the legacy window factories inside Tesserae's own trees** (probed: it works), the one temporary use of the old API, removed in M40 and caught by M43's `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED` gate. This keeps one build path. Alternative: keep `tre`'s `View` for any view containing one, which means two build paths and two binding systems until M40.
- Q3 **`tesserae.View`.** Recommended: **Tesserae's own class with the same surface Tesserae uses today** — `node(id)` returning a `tre` `Node`, `reconcile(spec=)`, `set_theme`/`set_stylesheet`, `instantiate` — plus `root`. Tests move to 0.3.4's names as they're touched (`get("text")` for `get_text()`), since 0.3.5 removes the old ones.
- Q4 **Proving parity.** Recommended: **a tree differ**. While 0.3.4 still has `tre`'s `View`, build every fragment (67) and every example view both ways and compare the two trees node by node through `get()` (kind, laid-out box, fill, stroke, corner radius, opacity, shadows, text and font), using `tre`'s `tools/dump_widget.py` output format when it arrives.

### Phase 1 — Tokens (from M38) ✅
- Step 1: `src/tesserae/tokens.py`, with values from `tre`'s source at v0.3.4. **Colour:** `color_scheme(seed, dark)` gives all 49 roles (materialyoucolor TonalSpot, `spec_version="2021"`, tone 10 for the four light `on_*_container` roles); `resolve_scheme` applies `tre`'s `View` seed precedence and `colors:` overrides, default theme then custom, raises on an unknown role, and returns `None` with no seed at all. **Shape** and **elevation** scales from `engine-md3`. **`elevation_shadows(level)`** turns `engine-render`'s key (30%) and ambient (15%) geometry into 0.3.4 `shadows`, key first since it paints on top; the one unavoidable difference is that the legacy ambient shadow keeps the node's corner radius while a `shadows` entry grows it by the spread. The 15 **type** roles. **`parse_color`**: hex (3, 4, 6, 8 digits), CSS names, `transparent`, and CSS Color 4 `rgb()`/`rgba()`/`hsl()`/`hsla()` in comma or space syntax with alpha; `tre` also takes CSS's wide-gamut functions (`color()`, `lab()`, `oklch()`, …), which raise "not supported by Tesserae yet". `materialyoucolor>=3.0.4,<4` added to the dependencies — ✅
- Step 2: `tests/test_tokens.py` (74 tests, all against `tre` 0.3.4): every role for 8 seeds, light and dark, against `Window.theme.role`; `colors:` overrides; seed precedence, including through real `View`s; every shape and elevation token through a `View`'s `corner_radius`/`elevation`; every type role against `Window.theme.typography` (compared at float32, which is how `tre` stores them); the shadow geometry, including level 1 against the pair `tre`'s own docs give, and that `tre` accepts it; 20 colour strings parsed as `tre` parses them, plus invalid and unsupported ones. Mutation-checked: `spec_version="2025"`, dropping the tone-10 rule, and the shadow alpha rounding each fail tests. 623 → 698 passed; design page updated; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 2 — Spec Compiler and Cascade ✅
- Step 1: `src/tesserae/spec/build.py`: an expanded spec to 0.3.4 nodes, following `tre`'s `build.rs`. **Kinds:** Rect/Container to `box` (a Rect needs `background`); Text to `text`; Link to `text` with `role="link"`, a pointer cursor and focus; TextField to a `box` (its background) holding a `text_input` (whose `fill` is the glyph colour); Image to `image` from Tesserae's decoded frames (1×1 transparent when there's no `src`); Icon to `path` from `tesserae/icons.py`, 12 icons extracted from `tre`'s `engine-md3` source. **The eight MD3 kinds** go through the legacy window factories (Q2), taken off the window root and given the node's layout, with `checked`/`selected`/`value`/`hour`/`minute` and `tre`'s wrong-state-field errors. **Layout, paint and text** map as `tre` maps them: every property is set explicitly, defaults included, except unset alignment, which `create` won't take as `None`. Theme roles, shape tokens and elevation (as `shadows`) resolve through `tesserae.tokens`, and text through the type scale, with explicit fields winning. Errors use `tre`'s messages. Tesserae now carries `tre`'s shipped default theme as `spec/default_theme.yaml`, the cascade's default layer — ✅
- Step 2: `src/tesserae/spec/cascade.py`: `tre`'s `resolve_style_layered`, with rules indexed by kind and id and class rules pre-sorted by count. It keeps `tre`'s quirk that a rule with several selectors applies at each tier it qualifies for on its own (a `kind:` + `classes:` rule applies at the kind tier even without the classes). `tests/test_spec_build.py` (42 tests): 11 cascade cases, the layering and the shipped default theme compared with a `tre` `View` on the same spec; fills from roles and colours, text, icons, text fields, images, the eight MD3 kinds with their state; 13 error cases whose messages equal `tre`'s exactly. Mutation-checked: class order, the id tier, the kind-tier quirk, inline styles, and the not-a-fill rule reading only inline `style:` (a survivor at first, until a test covered a Text under a baseline `background` rule). 698 → 741 passed — ✅

### Phase 3 — Tree Parity ✅
- Step 1: `tests/treediff.py`, the differ. It builds one spec with `tre`'s `View` + `Window.from_view` and with Tesserae's compiler in a same-sized window, lays both out, and compares every node through 0.3.4's `get()`: `layout_x/y/width/height`, `fill`, `stroke_color`/`stroke_width`, `corner_radius`, `opacity`, `elevation` (`tre`) against `shadows` (Tesserae), and text, font family, size, weight and line height. `tests/test_tree_parity.py`, the corpus: all **67 fragments** called with ordinary parameters (the Image fragment with a real PNG), **every view written inline in the suite that `tre` accepts** (49), the **5 example views** built from their files, and the **2 docs views**. **127 of 127 identical** — ✅
- Step 2: what the first run found, and the fixes (89 of 121 identical at first). A TextField draws its text in `tre`'s fixed baseline `#1C1B1F` (`engine-core`), which is also what its `fill` reads back, so Tesserae's `text_input` uses it. The MD3 kinds take the cascade's corner radius, border and shadows, not just layout. A `from_view` root with no height is sized to its content, so screens are mounted with `window.root` at `align_items="flex_start"`, which Phase 5's `App` needs too. SpinBox showed that Tesserae accepted a number as `text.content`; it now rejects one as `tre` does. **Not comparable** (`tre` can't read them back): a Link's text and font, and a TextField's background. Mutation-checked: dropping uniform padding (15 views fail), the MD3 kinds' paint (6) and font weight (39) are caught; a wrong level-5 shadow isn't, since no view uses `level_5`, but `test_tokens.py` pins it. 741 → 870 passed — ✅

### Phase 4 — View, Reconciler, Bindings ✅
- Step 1: `src/tesserae/view.py`, `View`: built by Tesserae's compiler into a given window, or into its own window, mounted and themed for the legacy kinds; `node(id)` (a TextField's `text_input`), `root`, `window`, `spec`. The compiler is split into per-kind props functions shared by `create` and a new `patch`, which sets a node's properties in place, resetting unset alignment to flexbox's defaults. **Reconciler:** `tre`'s rules (match children by id; patch only when kind, classes, style, text, image, icon or state changed; rebuild on a changed kind or root; destroy what's gone), plus ordering children as the new spec does with `insert_child`, which `tre` couldn't. `set_theme`/`set_stylesheet` re-patch every node without resetting a legacy widget's state. `ViewWatcher` reconciles a Tesserae `View` with its frames — ✅
- Step 2: bindings, `handlers:` and `two_way:` wired by Tesserae (moved from M36). Bindings are evaluated in a `tesserae.reactive` frame, re-tracked on each evaluation, and applied with `tre`'s type rules and messages; an unchanged value isn't set. Handlers: `on_click`/`on_hover_enter`/`on_hover_exit`/`on_change`/`on_focus_enter`/`on_focus_exit` map to `click`/`pointer_enter`/`pointer_leave`/`change`/`focus`/`unfocus`, a handler taking an argument gets the event, and other names are validated but not wired, as in `tre`. `two_way:` requires a plain `signal.get()`. **Found:** `node.on` keeps one listener per event, so each node and event gets one dispatcher that `on_change` and `two_way:` share. **Legacy MD3 widgets:** their `checked`/`selected`/`value` bindings are wired now through their legacy setters (the same temporary path as Q2, since they're `tre`'s widgets inside Tesserae's trees, not the `tre`-built nodes M36 found unreachable); M40 replaces them. Their single `set_on_change` slot fires on programmatic changes too, so it's suppressed while a binding sets their state. After any update, wiring is removed and redone without doubling listeners. `on_change` runs only for user edits. `tre`'s `_attach` and M35's bridge remain for views still built by `tre`'s `View` until Phase 6. `tests/test_view.py` (23 tests): bindings including `Computed`s and re-tracked dependencies; binding, handler and two-way errors equal to `tre`'s; handlers with and without the event; typing and toggling writing back, with `on_change` only for user edits; the reconciler keeping identity, bound values and focus, ordering, rebuilding and unsubscribing; restyling that keeps state; hot reload. **Bug found by a mutant:** `parent() is not outer` was always true (each `parent()` is a fresh handle), so every reconcile moved every child; it's `!=` now. Mutation-checked: no rewire, no legacy suppression, appending instead of ordering, and a removed node staying wired each fail tests; setting text when unchanged is invisible on 0.3.4 and not tested. 870 → 900 passed — ✅

### Phase 5 — Components and Screens ✅
- Step 1: `Component` (in `view.py`): an embedded view with its own ViewModel, built by `View.instantiate`/`Component.instantiate` in the host's window with the host's scheme and cascade layers, which **closes M31's embedded-component gap**. It follows the host when re-themed or re-styled, nested components included, and `remove()` unwires it and frees its nodes and any components inside. `tesserae.instantiate` uses it for a Tesserae parent. `Repeater` works unchanged, since it's built on `instantiate`/`remove`. A host's reconcile leaves its components in place. `View` now also takes a file path (built with `build_view_spec`, errors naming the file), as `tre`'s did, and gains `click(node)` for tests, via `window.simulate` — ✅
- Step 2: `App` creates its `tre.Window` up front, themed with the app's theme (the legacy MD3 kinds read it until M40), and builds screens into it with Tesserae's `View`. `show()` attaches the screen's root under `window.root` (no padding, `align_items="flex_start"`, as Phase 3 found `from_view` behaves) and detaches the previous one with `remove()`, which keeps it alive and wired; `Window.from_view`/`show_view` aren't used. `register()` takes a `tesserae.View`, rebuilds one made on its own into the app's window (`View.move_to`, keeping its ViewModel), and raises `TypeError` for a `tre` view. `tesserae.View`/`tesserae.Component` are now Tesserae's classes (Q3). Found along the way: a malformed theme or stylesheet used to be caught by `tre`'s schema, so the cascade now validates it (rules a list of mappings, known selector and style fields); colour errors use `tre`'s wording ("unknown color identifier", "wrong number of hex digits"); and theme tests read elevation back from `shadows` (`tests/helpers.py`), using MD3's 0–5 levels, since tre's own geometry is the same above 5. Tests pinned to `tre`-only behaviour were updated: `on_change` no longer fires on a re-style, and `App` no longer hands `tre` dicts. `tests/test_components_screens.py` (8 tests): components get and follow the host's theme and stylesheet, `remove`, nesting, a host reconcile, screen switching keeping hidden screens alive, re-homing a view built on its own, and rejecting a `tre` view. Mutation-checked: dropping the host's styling or the re-theme propagation each fail tests (the latter only after splitting a test that re-styled right after re-theming, which hid it). 900 → 913 passed; all 3 examples run on Tesserae-built views — ✅

### Phase 6 — Tests, Docs, Tracker ✅
- Step 1: **Production code off `tre`'s `View`:** `load_view` builds a `tesserae.View`; `instantiate` and `ViewWatcher` lose their `tre` fallbacks; `push_frames` (the legacy `push_frame`) is removed, since images go into `create("image", rgba=...)`; and **M35's recording bridge is removed** from `tesserae.reactive`, since no view uses `tre`'s binding evaluation any more (its two tests go with it). Two real bugs the switch-over found: a malformed theme's top-level keys, which `tre`'s schema used to catch, are now checked by Tesserae in `tre`'s words (`custom_theme_spec=: unknown field `colours``), naming the theme file; and **a reconcile is now all or nothing** — an invalid new root kind used to destroy the old tree before failing, so the new spec is built on the side first — ✅
- Step 2: **Tests:** the fragment tests (9 files, ~45 views) and `test_spec_expand` build Tesserae views (`tests/helpers.py`: `view_from`, and `elevation`, which reads a Tesserae node's level from its `shadows` and a legacy widget's from `elevation`); `test_images` checks the decoded pixels in the `image` node; `get_text()` (removed in 0.3.5) became `get("text")` on Tesserae nodes (19 uses); the examples use `window.simulate("click", ...)` instead of the legacy `window.click`. A TextField's `node(id)` is its text input, and its box (background, corners, border) is `node(id).parent()`, now documented. **Tests that still use `tre`'s `View` on purpose**, as the reference while 0.3.4 has it: `test_binding_parity`, `test_tokens`, `test_spec_build`, `test_tree_parity`/`treediff`, the error-parity checks in `test_view`, `test_components_screens`' rejection of a `tre` view, `test_reactive`'s `tre` half, and `test_thread_gc` (`tre` issue #10). M43's `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED` run must skip those. Imperative `tesserae.widgets` tests keep the legacy getters until M41. Full suite **909 passed** (with `TRE_SOURCE_DIR`), all 3 examples clean. Docs: `api/spec.md`, `architecture.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`, `guide/components.md`, and the module and example docstrings no longer describe `tre`'s `View`, `from_view`/`show_view` or the bridge; `mkdocs build --strict` clean. Tracker, artifact, `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md` — ✅

---

## Milestone 38 — MD3 Theme in Tesserae

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-25).** User: "push it and scope M38"; decisions Q1–Q4 made (below). `tre` D7: `tre` keeps no theme concept. Smaller than first planned: M37 Phase 1 brought the static tokens forward (colour roles, shape, elevation as `shadows`, the type scale, colour parsing), and live re-theming already works through `View.set_theme` and `App.set_theme_specs`.

**Scoped against the source and 0.3.4's API (2026-09-25):**
- **What's left of `tre`'s theme in Tesserae:** a theme's `components:` (per-component `corner_radius`/`elevation`, looked up as `"<component>.<variant>"` then `"<component>"`, field by field) and `typography:` (per-role field overrides; a custom theme's role entry replaces the default theme's) live only in the window's `ThemeState`, which `tre`'s imperative factories read — `tesserae.widgets` until M41. **In views, a theme's `typography:` does nothing**: a Text's `typography_role` resolves against the fixed scale in `tre` too (`engine_md3::type_style_named`), and Tesserae copied that.
- **Motion:** `tre`'s seven curves (`engine-core/src/animation.rs`). Standard (0.2, 0, 0, 1), StandardDecelerate (0, 0, 0, 1), StandardAccelerate (0.3, 0, 1, 1), EmphasizedDecelerate (0.05, 0.7, 0.1, 1) and EmphasizedAccelerate (0.3, 0, 0.8, 0.15) are single cubic béziers, which 0.3.4's `animate(easing=(x1, y1, x2, y2))` takes exactly. **Emphasized is two cubic segments** joined at x ≈ 0.167, which one bézier can't express.
- **Light and dark:** 0.3.4 delivers an OS switch as the window's `color_scheme` event (`dark`). `tre` also still flips the window's own theme on it (legacy widgets and hover tints follow), while Tesserae-built screens don't. So today an OS switch gives dark legacy widgets on light screens. **The OS's current appearance can't be read at startup**: `window.get` has only `width`, `height`, `title` and `scale_factor`.
- **Still needed until M40/M41:** `App` calls `Window.set_theme` for the legacy MD3 kinds and `tesserae.widgets`. Removing that belongs with M41, not here.

**Decisions for the user, each with a recommendation:**
- Q1 **Theme `typography:` in views.** **Decided (user, 2026-09-25):** apply it to **display text** — a Text's or Link's `typography_role`, and widget labels, resolve through the theme's overrides (default theme, then custom) — **but not to any text input**: single-line and multiline text fields and the code editor keep their own font (the code editor a monospace default), whatever the theme's `typography:` says. The user raised the exception ("there are exceptions, like the codeEditor or the multiline text field") and chose "every text input" over excluding only multiline fields and the editor. This differs from `tre`, where a theme's `typography:` is ignored in views; views with no `typography:` look the same.
- Q2 **Dark mode.** **Decided (user, 2026-09-25): `App(dark="system" | True | False)`, defaulting to `"system"`** ("default to system"), plus a runtime `App.set_dark(dark)`. `"system"` follows the OS through `color_scheme`, re-theming every screen and the window; with `True`/`False`, `App` re-asserts its own choice on `color_scheme`, so the legacy widgets no longer flip alone. **A default change:** until now an `App` was light unless told otherwise; tests and docs that assume light pass `dark=False`.
- Q3 **The OS appearance at startup.** **Decided (user, 2026-09-25): `"system"` starts dark** ("starting at dark is fine") and switches on the first `color_scheme` event, since 0.3.4 can't read the OS's appearance at startup. Asking `tre` for a readable `window.get("dark")` isn't needed for this; it would let `"system"` start right, and can be raised later.
- Q4 **Emphasized easing.** **Decided (user, 2026-09-25): MD3's single-bézier form, (0.2, 0, 0, 1)** ("that's fine"), as MD3's web tokens use; the difference from `tre`'s two-segment curve is written down.

### Phase 1 — Theme ✅
- Step 1: `src/tesserae/theme.py`, `tesserae.Theme.resolve(theme_seed, dark, default_theme_spec, custom_theme_spec)` (the default theme defaults to `tre`'s shipped one): `role`/`is_set` through `tokens.resolve_scheme`; `shape`/`elevation(component, variant)` from `components:`, with tokens resolved up front, looked up `component.variant` then `component` field by field, a custom entry replacing the default's for the same key, and `tre`'s wording for an unknown token; `typography(role)`, per-field overrides on the type scale, a custom role entry replacing the default's; `easing(name)` (MD3's seven curves as `animate` tuples, Emphasized as (0.2, 0, 0, 1) per Q4) and `duration(name)` (MD3's 16 tokens, short1 50 ms to extra_long4 1000 ms; `tre` has none). `app.theme`/`view.theme` return one. Exported as `tesserae.Theme` — ✅
- Step 2: `tests/test_theme_object.py` (110 tests): shape and elevation for every component key in the shipped default and a custom theme, plus variants, missing variants, unknown components and fallbacks, and typography for all 15 roles plus an unknown one, with the shipped default and with a custom theme — all compared with `tre`'s `Window.theme`; the replace-not-merge rules and the token error, also against `tre`; roles and `is_set`; malformed sections; motion tokens, including that `tre`'s `animate` takes every easing. Mutation-checked: no bare-component fallback (58 fail), merging a custom entry into the default's, and merging typography roles each fail tests. 909 → 1019 passed. Docs: `guide/themes-and-fonts.md` ("Reading the theme from code"), `api/index.md`, the design page; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 2 — Typography in Views ✅
- Step 1: the cascade's prepared layers (`spec.build.Layers`) now carry the two themes' `typography:` overrides (a custom role entry replacing the default's, validated), so every build and patch has them. A Text's or Link's `typography_role` resolves through them, while a TextField's doesn't (`_THEMED_TEXT`), per Q1; a node's own font fields still win. Re-theming (`View.set_theme`, `App.set_theme_specs`, theme hot reload) applies and removes overrides in place, and components follow their host. `tests/test_theme_typography.py` (8 tests): Text and Link follow, a TextField doesn't, explicit fields win, replace-not-merge, re-theming both ways, a component, an app's screens, a malformed entry. Mutation-checked: theming text inputs, merging roles, and ignoring the theme (`tre`'s behaviour) each fail tests. The tree-parity corpus is unaffected (none of its themes has `typography:`). 1019 → 1029 passed. Docs: "Typography in a theme" in `guide/themes-and-fonts.md`; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 3 — Light and Dark ✅
- Step 1: `App(dark="system" | True | False)`, defaulting to `"system"` (Q2), which starts dark (Q3); `App.dark` (what's showing) and `App.dark_mode`; `App.set_dark(True | False | "system")`. The app listens to the window's `color_scheme` event: following the OS, it re-themes every screen `load()`/`build_view()` made and the window (undoing on failure, as theme reloads do), logging the switch; with a fixed choice it puts the window's theme back, since `tre` flips it itself on an OS switch (the legacy widgets no longer flip alone). Screens loaded later and `app.theme` use the current appearance. No existing test assumed the old light default. `tests/test_dark_mode.py` (10 tests, OS switches through `window.simulate("color_scheme", dark=...)`): the default and its dark start; following the OS, screens and window, with bound values kept; both fixed choices ignoring the OS and keeping the window; `set_dark` both ways and back to `"system"`; later screens; `app.theme`; bad values. Mutation-checked: not re-asserting the window, starting light, and `set_dark` not re-theming each fail tests. 1029 → 1042 passed. Docs: "Light and dark" in `guide/themes-and-fonts.md` and `api/app.md` — ✅

### Phase 4 — Tests, Docs, Tracker ✅
- Step 1: docs were written with each phase (`guide/themes-and-fonts.md`: "Reading the theme from code", "Typography in a theme", "Light and dark"; `api/app.md`; `api/index.md`); this pass adds M38 to `ARCHITECTURE.md` and the design page. Known gaps: `"system"` starting dark until the first OS switch is listed, since `tre` 0.3.4 can't report the OS's appearance. M41 now carries dropping `App`'s `Window.set_theme` call once `tre`'s legacy widgets are gone. `mkdocs build --strict` clean; 1042 passed, all 3 examples clean. Tracker, artifact, `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md` — ✅

## Milestone 39 — Interaction: State Layer, Ripple, Focus, Accessibility

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 4 phases done (2026-09-25).** User: "Yes" (scope M39), then "Yes do as recommended" — Q1–Q3 as recommended. `tre` D8 and R12: `tre` draws no ripple, state layer, scrim or focus ring. Scoped from `tre`'s new `docs/design/legacy-behavior.md` (its M97 Phase 2 Step 2, on its local `0.3.5` branch, `9f926d6`) and probes on 0.3.4.

**What `tre`'s legacy interaction does** (the write-up, read from source):
- **Hover:** the node under the pointer (never its ancestors) animates a layer to 8% over 100 ms, linear.
- **Ripple:** every press spawns one at the press point, radius 0 → 100 px (a fixed size, not fitted to the node) and opacity 12% → 0 over 300 ms, linear, with no separate press and release phases.
- Both paint in the theme's `on_surface`, clipped to the node's rounded box.
- **Focus ring:** never painted.
- **Opt-in only:** no factory turns any of this on; a node gets it only from the app calling `enable_interaction()`, or from its first press, and then tinted black.

**A regression from M37, found scoping this:** in `tre`'s `View`, `handlers: {on_click: ...}` made a node focusable, a Tab stop, and activated by Enter (`set_on_click` added a click action). In Tesserae's `View`, `node.on("click")` doesn't: the same Rect isn't focusable, Tab skips it, and Enter does nothing. Probed side by side. Keyboard users can't reach YAML buttons until this is fixed. Accessibility is otherwise not worse: `tre`'s `View` nodes report no role through `get()`, and Tesserae's already set `role="link"` and `role="textbox"`.

**Decisions for the user, each with a recommendation:**
- Q1 **Clickable YAML nodes.** Recommended: **a node with an `on_click` handler becomes focusable, a Tab stop, and `role="button"` unless it has a role**, so Enter and Space activate it and assistive technology offers the action. That restores `tre`'s keyboard reachability, and the role goes further than `tre` did. Fix it first, as Phase 1.
- Q2 **Which numbers.** Recommended: **MD3's**, for Tesserae's widgets: hover 8%, focus 10%, pressed 10%, dragged 16%, in the content's colour; a ripple that covers the node, spreads while pressed and fades on release; and a visible focus ring on keyboard focus only (`focus_visible`), which `tre` never drew. The alternative is `tre`'s legacy numbers above (fixed 100 px ripple, no focus ring).
- Q3 **Plain YAML nodes.** Recommended: **a node with `on_click` gets the state layer and ripple automatically** (tinted `on_surface`, with `interaction: false` to opt out), so the button fragments feel like MD3 buttons. Alternatives: an explicit opt-in field, or none on YAML nodes (only on Tesserae's widgets, M40+).

### Phase 1 — Clickable Nodes Reachable ✅
- Step 1: the compiler makes a node whose spec has an `on_click` handler `focusable=True` and `role="button"` (Links, text fields and the legacy MD3 kinds keep their own role). A patch that drops `on_click` sets `focusable=False`, `role="none"` (0.3.4 won't take `role=None`), and the reconciler now patches when `handlers` change. Probed: a focusable `role="button"` box is a Tab stop, and Enter and Space each deliver `click`. `tests/test_clickable.py` (5 tests): Tab and Enter against `tre`'s `View` side by side, Space too, roles (a button, a plain node, a Link), Tab order skipping non-clickable nodes, adding and removing `on_click` by reconcile, and a button fragment wrapped as a clickable (a `component:` call takes no `handlers:`). Mutation-checked: not made reachable, not reset, and handlers not triggering a patch each fail tests. 1042 → 1048 passed. The known gap is fixed. Docs: `getting-started.md` — ✅

### Phase 2 — State Layer and Ripple ✅
- Step 1: `tesserae.interaction.Interaction`, built from 0.3.4's building blocks. The state layer is an absolute, non-hit-testable box added after the node's content, with the node set to `clip_children`. Its opacity is MD3's: dragged 16%, else focused 10% (keyboard focus only, `focus_visible`), else hovered 8%. Each press adds a ripple circle centred on the press point, sized to reach the farthest corner, at the pressed opacity (10%). The timing is Material Web's `md-ripple`: it grows from 20% of the node's larger side over 450 ms with the standard easing, fades in over 105 ms, fades out over 375 ms on release, and holds for at least 225 ms (an invisible `stroke_width` animation stands in for a timer, since `tre` has none). Enter and Space ripple from the centre; dragging a press off the node releases it; nested clickables ripple only the innermost. Probed first: hovering a child keeps the parent hovered, presses bubble with `target`, a keyboard click has no `x`, and `layout_width`/`layout_height` give the node's size. Per Q3 it applies to clickable `Rect`/`Container` nodes, tinted `on_surface` (MD3's baseline without a theme). The new `interaction:` field takes `false` (opt out), `{color: ...}` (a role or hex) or `true` (without `on_click`); other values and non-box kinds are errors. `View._sync_interactions` adds, removes and re-tints after every build, reconcile, re-theme and re-style; the listeners share the per-node dispatcher (`View._listen`) but aren't unwired with the ViewModel; `Component.remove` and `move_to` detach them. `View.interaction(id)` exposes it. `tests/test_interaction.py` has 16 tests. Mutation-checked with 8 mutants (no nearest check, `focus_visible` ignored, no minimum press, no re-tint, leave keeps the press, radius from the centre, layer first, clip not restored); each fails a test. 1048 → 1068 passed (the run gave 1064; the new guide's two YAML blocks then added 4 doc checks, found in Phase 3). Docs: new `guide/interaction.md`, and updates to `getting-started.md`, `themes-and-fonts.md`, `api/app.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 3 — Focus Ring and Accessibility ✅
- Step 1: MD3's focus ring on keyboard focus (`focus_visible`), in `Interaction`: 3 px in `secondary` (MD3's baseline without a theme), 2 px outside the node, its corners grown by 5 px (each corner, for per-corner radii; square stays square). It's a stroked box at −5 px, because a box's stroke is drawn inside it (`tre`'s paint docs). It's placed again whenever focus arrives, hidden on `unfocus`, and never shown for pointer focus. For the ring to show outside the node, the node is no longer clipped: an inner clip box that follows the node's corners holds the state layer and ripples, which lifts Phase 2's clipping limit. All decoration nodes are `a11y_hidden`. `tesserae.a11y` gives `ROLES`/`LIVE`/`ACTIONS` (probed from 0.3.4's errors: `click` isn't an `a11y_action`), `check`/`describe` (every field checked before any is set; `hidden` becomes `a11y_hidden`) and `on_action` (per-action routing, on a view's shared dispatcher or alone). The YAML `a11y:` field takes `label`, `role`, `hidden`, `live` and `level`. `role` overrides a clickable's `button` and isn't allowed on Links or TextFields; a TextField's fields go on its input; a patch resets dropped fields; errors name the widget. Probed: `tre`'s `disabled` is announced only (the node still focuses and clicks), so it's left to M40's controls. `tests/test_a11y.py` has 24 tests, and 7 Phase 2 tests were updated for the clip box. Mutation-checked with 11 mutants; one (the ring not re-placed on focus) survived until a test resized a node outside a reconcile; now each fails a test. 1068 → 1094 passed. Docs: the guide is now `Interaction & Accessibility`, with the `api/index.md` entries (and its stale "View is still `tre`'s" paragraph fixed), and `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 4 — Tests, Docs, Tracker ✅
- Step 1: the closing pass. Tests (`simulate`/`advance`, as M34's spike) and docs were written phase by phase: `test_clickable.py` (5), `test_interaction.py` (16) and `test_a11y.py` (25), each mutation-checked, and the guide `Interaction & Accessibility`. The pass added: `a11y:` labels on every example button ("Increment", "Open settings", "Back to home", "Add item", "Remove item"), and `interaction: {color: "#FFFFFF"}` on the filled purple and red ones, since MD3 tints a filled button in its content colour; all three examples run cleanly. A test drives the counter example by keyboard alone, through `App`: Tab, ring, role and label, then Enter and Space. `tests/treediff.py` gives `tre`'s builder a copy of each spec without `a11y`/`interaction` (`for_tre`), since `tre` rejects fields it never had. `docs/index.md` lists the feature. 1094 → 1095 passed — ✅

## Milestone 40 — Widgets I: Stateful Controls

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 6 phases done (2026-09-25).** User: "Push it and go with your recommendations for M40" — Q1–Q4 as recommended (MD3/HTML behaviour, MD3's look, state as `Signal`s, Carousel and Splitter to M42). Rebuilds the stateful MD3 controls `tre` M99 deletes, from 0.3.4's building blocks, on M39's `tesserae.interaction` and `tesserae.a11y`. The M34 design settled the shape (P6): a stateful control is a small Tesserae object holding `.node` and its state, and a `tesserae.widgets` factory returns one instead of a bare `tre.Node` (the one written break). Scoped from `tre`'s `docs/design/legacy-behavior.md` (its `0.3.5` branch), the M34 spike `tools/spikes/checkbox_on_primitives.py`, and probes on 0.3.4.

**What exists today:**
- **YAML kinds:** eight YAML kinds are still `tre`'s legacy factories, moved into Tesserae's tree (`build._legacy`): `Checkbox`, `RadioButton`, `Switch`, `Slider`, `CircularProgress`, `LinearProgress`, `LoadingIndicator` and `TimePickerDial`. They're driven only through `set_checked`/`set_selected`/`set_on_change`.
- **Fragments:** the matching fragments sit in `spec/components/`. `SpinBox_Component.yaml` is a composition with no behaviour.
- **Factories:** `tesserae.widgets` has `checkbox`, `radio_button`, `switch`, `slider`, `spin_box`, `circular_progress`, `linear_progress`, `loading_indicator` and `time_picker_dial`, each delegating to `tre`'s `add_*`.
- **Link:** already Tesserae's own (M37: a `text` with `role="link"`), so it needs no work here.
- **Carousel and Splitter:** never wrapped by Tesserae at all.

**Found scoping this (probed on 0.3.4):**
- **Inert controls:** clicking a YAML `Checkbox`, `Switch` or `RadioButton` never toggles it, and Tab skips all three (not focusable, no role). `tre`'s own `View` and its `add_checkbox` behave the same: "the engine never toggles them" (`legacy-behavior.md`). So the todo_list example's checkbox can't be ticked. Only the `Slider` is focusable (`role="slider"`, arrows move it).
- **`disabled`:** `tre`'s `disabled` is announced only; the node still focuses and clicks (M39 Phase 3).

**Decisions for the user, each with a recommendation:**
- Q1 **Behaviour.** Recommended: **controls behave as MD3 and HTML controls do.** A click, Space or Enter toggles a checkbox or switch and selects a radio. A slider drags (pointer capture) and steps with the arrow keys, Page Up/Down and Home/End, and answers `a11y_action` increment/decrement/set_value. Each fires `on_change`. `two_way:` keeps a `Signal` in step; a one-way binding sets the control whenever its `Signal` changes, so the app can overrule a user's toggle. `disabled` blocks focus and input, with MD3's disabled look. Alternative: keep `tre`'s inert controls, with the app toggling them in `on_click`.
- Q2 **Look.** Recommended: **MD3's specification, as with M39's numbers.**
  - Sizes and target: MD3's fixed sizes (checkbox 18 px, radio 20 px, switch 52×32 with a 16/24/28 px handle, a slider with a 4 px track and a 20 px handle), drawn centred in the node, whose box is the touch target (48 px by default).
  - Colours: roles (`primary`, `on_primary`, `outline`, `on_surface`, `surface_container_highest`), with the fragments' `background:` kept as an override for the selected colour.
  - Animations: MD3's motion tokens.
  - Progress: indeterminate linear and circular progress added, since MD3 has them and `tre` didn't.
  - Loading indicator: the handover's defined shapes (not the diamonds `tre` actually drew).
  Alternative: `tre`'s legacy drawing, scaled to the node's box.
- Q3 **State API.** Recommended: **each control's state is a Tesserae `Signal`** (`checkbox.checked`, `slider.value`, `dial.hour`/`dial.minute`), so Python code reads it, sets it and binds `Effect`s to it, and YAML bindings go straight to it. Alternative: plain properties with an `on_change` callback.
- Q4 **Carousel and Splitter.** Tesserae never exposed them, so they would be new widgets, not a migration. Recommended: **move them to M42**, beside docking, where a splitter belongs. Alternative: build them here.

### Phase 1 — Control Foundation ✅
- Step 1: `tesserae.controls.Control`, proved on `Checkbox`. Structure: `.node` is a 48 px focusable target with the role. A 40 px circle inside it is the `Interaction`'s new `surface` (events from the target, feedback in the circle; a ripple's press point is converted, since `layout_x` is window-wide). The state (`Checkbox.checked`) and `disabled` are `Signal`s, and one `Effect` repaints; the first paint doesn't animate. Behaviour: `on_change` hears only the user's changes. `disabled` sets `focusable=False`, announces `disabled`, ignores clicks, and turns the feedback off (`Interaction.enabled`, new). `set_theme` re-tints at once, which needs `set`, not `animate(..., 0)`, since the latter waits a frame. Colours: roles, or `tokens.BASELINE` (new: MD3's published colours from `tre`'s handover, plus `on_surface`/`secondary`; `build._BASELINE` now reuses it). Listeners: `tesserae.listeners.Listeners` is the shared dispatcher, taken out of `View._listen`, which delegates to it; a control takes `listen=` to share a view's. The checkbox: 18 px, a 2 px `on_surface_variant` outline; `primary` with an `on_primary` check drawn in by `trim_end` (`medium1`, emphasized decelerate; out `short3`, emphasized accelerate); the state layer `primary` when checked, else `on_surface`; disabled in `on_surface` at 38% with a `surface` check; `color=` overrides `primary`. `tests/test_controls.py` has 19 tests. Mutation-checked with 10 mutants; each fails a test. 1095 → 1114 passed. Docs: `api/index.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md`; the controls guide comes in Phase 6. — ✅

### Phase 2 — Selection Controls ✅
- Step 1: (`Checkbox` was built in Phase 1.) `RadioButton`: a 20 px ring, 2 px wide, in `on_surface_variant`; selected, it turns `primary` and a 10 px dot grows in with `scale` (`medium1`, emphasized decelerate). A click selects it, and it never deselects itself. `RadioGroup` keeps one selected (the app setting one does the same) and is one Tab stop: the selected button, else the first enabled one. The Tab stop is a roving focus via a new `Control._focusable()`. The arrow keys move the selection and focus, wrap, skip disabled buttons, and fire `on_change`; a destroyed button leaves its group. `Switch`: a 52×48 target (`Control.target`, new) holding a 52×32 track. Off, it's `surface_container_highest` with a 2 px `outline` border and a 16 px `outline` handle; on, it's `primary` with a 24 px `on_primary` handle; pressed, the handle is 28 px. The handle is drawn at 28 px and sized by `scale`, and it slides 20 px with `translate_x` (`medium2`), since tre won't animate `width` or `x` (probed); the state-layer circle rides with it. The focus ring goes around the track (new `Interaction(ring_around=)`). Disabled uses on_surface at 12% for the track, `surface` for the on-handle, and 38% for the off-handle. `a11y` `checked` carries the state for both. 13 new tests in `tests/test_controls.py` (32 in all). Mutation-checked with 11 mutants; each fails a test. 1114 → 1127 passed. Docs: `api/index.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 3 — Slider and Spin Box ✅
- Step 1: `Slider`: a `width`×48 target (200 by default); a 4 px track, `primary` to the value and `surface_container_highest` after it; a 20 px `primary` handle whose centre travels `width − 20`. Its state-layer circle rides the handle. Pressing sets the value and captures the pointer, so a drag can leave the slider (probed: after `capture_pointer`, moves and the release arrive with `x` relative to the slider, however far outside). The drag shows MD3's dragged 16% layer, and `on_change` fires once at release if the value changed. Arrows step it, Page Up/Down by ten steps, Home/End to the ends; `a11y_action` increment/decrement/set_value, clamped. The value is clamped and snapped, rounding half up as HTML's range input does (Python's `round` goes to even; a test caught it). `value`/`value_min`/`value_max`/`value_step` are announced, and the handle moves at once, not animated. `SpinBox`: two 40 px icon buttons (`remove`/`add`, `role="button"`, "Decrease"/"Increase", each with its own `Interaction`) around a 64×40 `surface_container_highest` field holding a `text_input` (probed: `change` fires on every edit). The buttons, up/down arrows in the field and `a11y_action` increment/decrement step it. Typing sets the value once the text parses within the bounds; leaving the field puts back the value's text. A button whose step would pass a bound is disabled. Integer steps show integers, fractional ones `:g`. It isn't a `Control` (three targets) but has the same API. 15 new tests (47 in all). Mutation-checked with 12 mutants; each fails a test. 1127 → 1142 passed. Docs: `api/index.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 4 — Progress, Loading, Time Dial ✅
- Step 1: The indicators share an `Indicator` base: `role="progressbar"`, not focusable, `value` a `Signal`, 0..1 or `None` for indeterminate. The loop chains `on_complete`, with a generation counter; one mutant, dropping the counter's bump on settling, survived because it's equivalent: settling's `stop_animation` already ends the loop, since tre never completes a stopped animation. `LinearProgress`: a 4 px `surface_container_highest` track that clips a full-width `primary` bar slid by `translate_x` (`width` doesn't animate), easing to a new value over `medium1`. Indeterminate, a 40% bar sweeps again and again (MD3's two-bar sweep, simplified to one). `CircularProgress`: a 4 px `primary` arc in 48 px, a `path` circle clockwise from 12 o'clock with `trim_end` = value. Indeterminate, it spins once per 1568 ms while the arc stretches between 0.1 and 0.75 (666 ms). `LoadingIndicator`: a 38 px `primary` shape in 48 px, morphing through the handover's pentagon, pill, cookie and oval (its defined curved pill and oval, not the diamonds tre drew), 650 ms per step. Probed: `animate("data")` morphs any two outlines, resampling them itself, and `path` takes arcs and curves. `TimePickerDial` (a `Control`, 256 px, `role="slider"`): a `surface_container_highest` face; the 12 hours or the minutes in fives as text; a 2 px `primary` hand (a `path`) to a 48 px `primary` selector whose number turns `on_primary`; the selector and its state-layer circle move by `translate_x`/`translate_y`. A press or drag (with pointer capture) points the active hand: the hour snaps to 12 and keeps AM/PM, the minute snaps to 5. Releasing in hour mode moves to minutes (`auto_advance`). Arrows and `a11y_action` step by 1 hour or 5 minutes; the ring goes around the face; `on_change` gets `(hour, minute)`. Found: tre reads path `data` back normalised, so tests compare numbers, not strings. 15 new tests (62 in all). Mutation-checked with 12 mutants: 11 caught (one needed a stronger test), 1 equivalent. 1142 → 1157 passed. Docs: `api/index.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 5 — Wiring ✅
- Step 1: The eight YAML kinds compile to Tesserae's controls (`build._control`), and `build._legacy`/`_legacy_state` are gone (`_LEGACY_KINDS` → `_CONTROL_KINDS`). A control's style gives its size, its placement (margins, flex, position; not its own alignment) and its colour (`background`, or `foreground` for the indicators). `LoadingIndicator` no longer needs a width. `Built.controls` holds them and `View.control(id)` returns one. They share the view's `Listeners`; a trial build gets its own and disposes its controls. Bindings on `checked`/`selected`/`value`/`hour`/`minute`/`disabled` set the control's `Signal`s, coerced with `tre`'s messages. `on_change` and `two_way:` hear the control's `on_change`, so M37's legacy change-slot suppression (`_add_legacy_change`, `_legacy_set`, `_applying`) is gone. A reconcile patches a control (placement, colour, theme, the YAML's state) or rebuilds it when its size or `group:` changes (`control_shape`). A re-theme or re-style keeps what the user did. Forgotten, moved and removed controls are disposed (`Control.dispose()`, new). New: a `RadioButton` `group:` field, where the same name in a view means one `RadioGroup`. The eight control fragments' notes are rewritten; the `SpinBox` fragment stays a composition (named). `tesserae.widgets`' nine stateful factories return controls (P6), with a migration note in the widget catalog: they're placed on the root and at `x`/`y`, take `theme=`/`label=`, and `spin_box` returns a `SpinBox`. The todo_list example ticks its checkbox by a real click. Tests: `tests/test_controls_in_views.py` (14), plus build, view, fragment and factory tests moved from `tre`'s getters to the controls; `treediff` skips control kinds (MD3's look by Q2, not `tre`'s) and strips `group`. Mutation-checked with 11 mutants: 10 caught (one after a test was tightened), 1 equivalent (the trial's own dispatcher, since disposing already cleans up). 1157 → 1179 passed. Docs: widget catalog, component fragments, interaction, API index, design doc, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

## Milestone 41 — Widgets II: Composed Catalog and Overlays

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 6 phases done (2026-09-25).** User: "Push it and go with your recommendations for M41" — Q1–Q4 as recommended (built from the fragments, MD3's behaviour, `tesserae.overlays`, the window-theme removal at M42's end). Rebuilds the composed MD3 factories and the overlays that `tre` M99 deletes, on 0.3.4's building blocks, M39's interaction and M40's controls. Scoped from `src/tesserae/widgets` (a scan of its `tre` calls), the fragments in `spec/components/`, `tre`'s `legacy-behavior.md` (0.3.5), and 0.3.4's `show_layer`/`hide_layer`.

**What exists today:**
- **Factories:** `tesserae.widgets` still delegates 36 functions to `tre`.
  - M41's share: `button`, `icon_button`, `fab`, `extended_fab`, `split_button`, `button_group`, `card`, `chip`, `badge`, `divider`, `list_`, `list_item`, `accordion_header`, `tree_node`, `link`, `icon`, `tabs`, `navigation_rail`, `navigation_drawer`, `toolbar`, `top_app_bar`, `status_bar`, `dialog`, `menu`/`menu_item` (`build_menu`), `snackbar`, `tooltip` and `side_sheet`.
  - M42's share: search, date and time, `video`, `node_graph`/`graph_node`, pagination, popovers and docking. (`image` uses `add_image_from_bytes`, which D6 keeps.)
- **Fragments:** 67 already build these widgets from primitives with Tesserae's compiler, matched against `tre`'s factories when they were written (M17–M27).
  - They cover every fixed-shape widget here.
  - The list-driven ones (`tabs`, `navigation_rail`, `navigation_drawer`, `button_group`, `list_`) have none.
  - Fragments can't take `handlers:`, and they have no behaviour.
- **Icons:** Tesserae already has `tre`'s 12 icons (`tesserae.icons`, M37), so the old Step 2 here is done.
- **Overlays:** `tre` 0.3.4 gives `show_layer(node, anchor=, placement=, modal=, dismissible=)`, `hide_layer`, a `dismiss` event, modality and a Tab scope. `tre`'s legacy overlays closed themselves on the dismissals they allowed (`legacy-behavior.md` has the table), and never flipped to fit the window.
- **`legacy-behavior.md`** notes two corner morphs and a bug:
  - `split_button` tightens its facing corners on hover (100 ms);
  - `button_group` tightens the pressed child's corners and reflows its row;
  - that reflow compounds on every layout pass and never restores, so the intent should be rebuilt, not the bug.
- **A conflict in the plan:** the old Step 3 here (stop `App` calling `Window.set_theme`) can't happen in M41, because M42's widgets are still `tre`'s and read the window's theme.

**Decisions for the user, each with a recommendation:**
- Q1 **How to build them.** Recommended: **from the fragments, through Tesserae's compiler.** A factory expands its fragment with its arguments and builds it, so the YAML and Python paths share one definition. The list-driven widgets are built in Python from item fragments. Each factory returns a small `Widget` object: `.node`, `.part(id)`, `on_click(fn)`, and `set_theme`. That's the same kind of break as M40's: a composed factory stops returning a bare `tre.Node`, though `.node` is one. The fragments are audited against MD3's specification as each is moved. Alternative: hand-written Python per widget, as M40's controls are.
- Q2 **Behaviour.**
  - Recommended: **MD3's**: the state layer, ripple and focus ring on every interactive part (buttons, chips, list items, menu items, tabs, navigation items).
  - Selection state as `Signal`s for tabs, the navigation rail and drawer, and the button group, with the arrow keys moving it; expand/collapse for accordion headers and tree nodes.
  - The split-button and button-group morphs rebuilt as intended.
  - Alternative: static widgets, as `tre`'s factories were.
- Q3 **Overlays.** Recommended: **`tesserae.overlays`**: `Dialog`, `Menu` (anchored, and a context menu at the pointer on `secondary_click`), `Snackbar`, `Tooltip`, `SideSheet` and a modal `NavigationDrawer`. Each has `open()`/`close()` on `show_layer`, closes itself on the dismissals `tre`'s legacy overlays allowed, and has an `on_close` callback. MD3 timing is added where `tre` had none: a snackbar hides itself after 4 s unless `duration=None`, and a tooltip opens after a 500 ms hover. Placement flips to fit, as `show_layer` does. Alternative: `tre`'s exact behaviour (no timers).
- Q4 **The window's theme.** Recommended: **move "`App` stops calling `Window.set_theme`" to the end of M42**, when the last `tre`-drawn widget goes. M41 keeps it, and M43's gate checks it's gone. Alternative: keep it in M41 and drop window theming for M42's widgets early.

### Phase 1 — Foundation ✅
- Step 1: `tesserae.widgets._composed.Widget`: a fragment expanded with a factory's arguments (`expand_components_to_spec`) and built as a `View` into the window, then attached to the root and placed at `x`/`y`. It has `.node`, `.part(name)`, `.interaction(part)`, `on_click(fn, part=)` (the part becomes a focusable `role="button"`; Enter and Space click it; it returns a stopper), `set_theme(theme)` (new `View._use_scheme`) and `destroy()`. The parts a factory names get M39's feedback in their content's colour (`interaction: {color: ...}`). With no theme it uses `tokens.baseline_scheme()` (new: all 49 roles, the baseline seed's scheme with MD3's published baseline over it), since fragments name roles that need a scheme. `button` is proved on it: five variants (elevated, filled, filled tonal, outlined, text), a pill unless `corner_radius=`, and `border_color`/`border_width`, `theme=` and `on_click=`. Found while auditing the fragments against MD3: their labels were 0 px wide and uncentred, since tre 0.3.4's text has no intrinsic size (the "later enhancement" M37 named). Now the compiler sizes a `Text`/`Link` with no width or height to its content (`build.natural_size`, via `window.measure_text`; tre's `line_height` is a multiple of the font size), a bound `text` is re-measured, and the five button fragments centre their label. `treediff` gives tre's copy the same measured sizes, so parity still holds. Also found and fixed a bug from M37 Phase 4 (`055737c`): `patch` passed the alignment defaults and the node's props as two `**` expansions, so re-theming, re-styling or reconciling any node whose style set `align_items`/`justify_content` raised `TypeError`; a regression test covers it. `tests/test_composed_widgets.py` has 14 tests, plus 3 in `test_view.py`; the 3 `button`-vs-`add_button` parity tests are gone. Mutation-checked with 8 mutants; one survived until a test built after a re-colour; now each fails a test. 1183 → 1198 passed. Docs: widget catalog, component fragments, API index, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 2 — Buttons and Actions ✅
- Step 1: `icon_button` (standard, filled, filled tonal, outlined; a circle; `label=`). `fab` (surface, primary, secondary, tertiary; MD3's small 40/12, default 56/16 and large 96/28 with a 36 px icon). `extended_fab` (56 px tall; without an icon, the label alone with 20 px either side). `split_button` (`leading` and `trailing` parts, `on_click`/`on_menu`; while hovered, the facing corners tighten from the pill to 8 px, or the theme's `split_button.tightened`, over 100 ms, and the feedback's clip follows). `button_group` (list-driven, so its spec is built from button fragments with `_composed.fragment`; parts `b0`..; `on_click(index)`; the pressed button's corners tighten to 8/12/16 by height and it grows 12 px while its neighbours share the loss, restoring on release or when dragged off). The reflow is set from the resting width, since tre won't animate width, so it can't compound as tre's did. All are built from their fragments, with feedback in their content's colour (new `_composed.content_role`); `Widget` also takes a prebuilt `spec=`. The fragments were audited against MD3: the icon button, FAB and split button fragments now centre their content, and the standard and outlined icon buttons' icon is `on_surface_variant` (tre drew `primary`). Probed: the parent gets `pointer_enter` when the pointer lands straight on a child, and `pointer_leave` fires while a press is held. `test_widgets_buttons.py` (tre-parity) is replaced by 14 behaviour tests in `test_composed_widgets.py` (27 in all); the fragment tests read `.node`/`.part`. Mutation-checked with 12 mutants: 11 caught (one needed its break redone, and the split test was tightened to click each part alone), 1 equivalent (`release()` before a press, which one pointer can't reach). 1198 → 1205 passed. Docs: widget catalog, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 3 — Containment and Lists ✅
- Step 1: All built from their fragments. `card`: elevated, filled, outlined; feedback and a button role only with `on_click`. `chip`: assist, filter, input, suggestion; 32 px, padded 16 (8 beside an icon), optional leading `icon`. A filter chip toggles `.selected` (a `Signal`) on click, Space or Enter, as `role="checkbox"`; selected, it's `secondary_container` with the check inserted and no border, and it keeps that through a re-theme (new `Widget.after_theme`). An input chip with `removable=True` has a close button, "Remove <label>", calling `on_remove`. `badge`: a 6 px `error` dot, or a 16 px pill fitted to its `label_small` label. `divider`: `outline_variant`, either orientation, hidden from assistive technology. `link`. `icon` (Tesserae's own; decorative unless `label=`, then `role="img"`). `list_item`: 56 px, or 72 with `supporting_text` (`body_medium`, `on_surface_variant`); 24 px icons either side; parts `headline`/`supporting`/`leading`/`trailing`. `list_`: `role="list"`, its items `listitem`; empty is an error, as tre's was. `accordion_header` and branch `tree_node`: `.expanded` (a `Signal`) and `.on_change`, toggled by click, Enter or Space; `a11y_action` expand/collapse; the chevron turns (accordion 0°→180°, tree −90°→0°); a tree node's right and left arrows set it. Tree nodes are `treeitem` at `level` depth+1, indented 16 + 24 per depth. `Widget.on_click` now gives any part feedback on demand (`interactive`) and takes a `role`. Fragment audit: the chips centre and pad their content, and the list item's headline is MD3's `body_large` (tre used `label_large`). Found and fixed, older than M41: tre 0.3.4's `text` never gets pointer events, so a YAML `Link` (a bare `text` since M37) could only be clicked from the keyboard. A Link is now a box (events, focus, `role="link"`, its text as its label) holding its text; handlers go on the box, `view.node(id)` returns it, and a label reset on patch no longer clears it. A regression test in `test_clickable.py` covers it. Tests: 17 new in `test_composed_widgets.py` (44 in all), plus the Link test; `test_widgets_structural.py` (tre-parity) is gone, and the fragment, expand and typography tests are updated. Mutation-checked with 12 mutants; each fails a test. 1205 → 1209 passed (the 14 tre-parity tests went with their file). Docs: widget catalog, component fragments, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 4 — Navigation ✅
- Step 1: `tabs`, the navigation rail and the navigation drawer have no fragments, so their specs are built in Python; they share `navigation._Selection` (`.selected` a `Signal` index or `None`, `.on_change`, click or Enter selects, one roving Tab stop, wrapping arrow keys, `role="tab"` items with `selected`, restyled after a re-colour). `tabs` is MD3's primary tabs: 48 px (64 with icons), `title_small` labels (`primary` selected, `on_surface_variant` otherwise), a 3 px `primary` indicator as wide as the label that slides (`translate_x`, `medium2`, emphasized), a `surface_variant` divider, and a `tablist` row. `navigation_rail`: 80 px of `surface`, items a 24 px icon in a 56×32 pill (`secondary_container` and `on_secondary_container` when selected) over a `label_medium` label. `navigation_drawer`: 360 px of `surface_container_low`, 12 px in, 56 px items with a full-width 28 px-radius `secondary_container` pill when selected; `modal=True` gives the modal drawer's corners, rounded on its end side and kept through a re-colour, and opening it as an overlay is Phase 5's. `toolbar` (from its fragments): docked (64 px, full width) or floating (a pill with elevation, horizontal or vertical), `surface_container` or vibrant `primary_container`, `role="group"`, for the app's icon buttons. `top_app_bar` (from its fragment): 64 px of `surface` with a `title_large` title and 48 px icon buttons (the leading in `on_surface`, trailing in `on_surface_variant`) as parts with feedback. `status_bar` (from its fragment), announced politely. `Widget` now runs a factory's `edit` before marking its `interactive` parts, so parts it adds can be interactive. tre's own 150×48 tabs, 80×52 rail items and 356 px drawer were measured, then MD3's numbers used. `test_widgets_navigation.py` (tre-parity) is replaced by 12 behaviour tests (54 in `test_composed_widgets.py`); the navigation fragment tests read `.node`. Mutation-checked with 10 mutants; three survived (the indicator's slide, the trailing icons' role, re-theming tabs) until their tests were tightened; now each fails a test. 1209 → 1214 passed. Docs: widget catalog, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 5 — Overlays ✅
- Step 1: `tesserae.overlays` on `show_layer`/`hide_layer`. Probed first: an anchored layer flips to fit; `dismiss` comes on Escape and on an outside press, and the layer stays open until hidden; hiding returns focus; a modal layer takes focus and keeps Tab inside it; animations run on a node in no tree (so they serve as timers). `Overlay` (open/close/`is_open`/`on_close`/`set_theme`) closes itself on `dismiss`; a modal overlay's full-window scrim takes outside presses, leaving Escape, as tre's legacy table has it. `Dialog`: its fragment plus right-aligned text-button `actions`, each calling its function and closing; `role="dialog"` named by its headline. `Menu`: MD3's `surface_container` panel (4 px corners, elevation 2) of 48 px `menuitem`s, either `(label, fn)` pairs or `menu_item` widgets (moved in, keeping their own dispatcher); focus moves to the first item, the arrows wrap, and click or Enter chooses and closes; `open(anchor)`, `open_at(x, y)`, `attach_context(node)` on `secondary_click`. `Snackbar`: 24 px in and 72 px up, not dismissible, an `inverse_primary` action and a "Close" icon button, `role="alert"` announced politely, hiding itself after 4 s (`duration=None` keeps it) via `_Timer`. `Tooltip`: fitted to its text, below its anchor 500 ms after hover or at once on keyboard focus, hidden on leave or unfocus, naming its anchor. `SideSheet` and `NavigationDrawer` (modal, over a scrim) slide in from the end or start (`medium4`, emphasized decelerate); the drawer closes when an item is chosen. `Widget` takes `attach=False`. `tesserae.widgets`' `dialog`, `snackbar`, `side_sheet` (modal is `SideSheet`, standard a `Widget`), `menu`, `menu_item` and `tooltip` move onto them. `test_widgets_overlays.py` (tre-parity) is replaced by `tests/test_overlays.py` (20); the fragment tests read `.node`. Mutation-checked with 12 mutants: 11 caught, 1 equivalent (the timer's generation check, since cancelling stops the animation and tre never completes a stopped one). 1214 → 1227 passed. Docs: new `guide/overlays.md`, widget catalog, API index, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 6 — Tests, Docs, Tracker, Gate ✅
- Step 1: The per-widget tests were written phase by phase: `test_composed_widgets.py` (54) and `test_overlays.py` (20), each mutation-checked, replacing four tre-parity files. The gate: `tests/test_no_tre_controls.py` now also forbids M41's 28 factories and tre's `open_*`/`close_*`, `build_menu` and `set_active_tab`; `src/tesserae` uses none.
