## Milestone 42 — Widgets III: Search, Date and Time, Media, the Node Graph, the Rest, Off the Window's Theme

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 8 phases done (2026-09-26).** User: Q1 "Build them all in M42"; Q2, Q3 and Q4 as recommended. The last of the migration (the eight `tesserae.widgets` functions still delegated to `tre`, and `App` off `Window.set_theme`) and, by Q1, the widgets `tre` has that Tesserae never wrapped. Docking and the app shell go to M45, after M43 (Q2). Scoped from `tre`'s removal list (`python/tre/_removed.py`, 0.3.5), its `target-api.md` and 0.3.4's factory docs.

**What's left:**
- **Delegated functions:** `search_bar`, `search_view`, `date_picker_day`, `period_selector`, `video`, `node_graph`, `graph_node` and `image`. All eight are on the removal list; `image`'s `add_image_from_bytes` is too, though D6 said it would stay.
- **The window's theme:** `App._set_window_theme` still calls `Window.set_theme`, and 46 lines in six fragment-test helpers (`_themed_window`) theme windows for `tre`'s legacy widgets. Under the shim that's 219 failures, plus one `Window.theme` read.
- **What `tre`'s versions do:**
  - `add_search_bar` returns `(bar, text_field, leading, trailing)`.
  - `add_search_view` is an empty unattached panel the app fills and opens with `open_menu`.
  - `add_node_graph` is a pannable, zoomable viewport. `add_graph_node` is a titled node inside it, with no drag and no edges ("draw edges with a canvas").
- **Never wrapped, so new rather than migration:** `tre` also has `time_input_field`, `pagination`, `popover`, `segmented_button`, `carousel` and `splitter` (the last two moved here by M40 Q4), and `build_shell`.
- **Docking can't be built on 0.3.4:** its framework-side API is 0.3.5's (`dock_target`/`dock_drop` window events and `set_active_panel`), while 0.3.4 has only the calls 0.3.5 removes (`set_dock_handle`, `drag_panel_over`, ...). Tesserae never exposed docking or `build_shell`, so M99 doesn't wait on either.

**Decisions for the user, each with a recommendation:**
- Q1 **Scope.** Recommended: **M42 is the migration only**: the eight functions, `App` off the window's theme, and the tests' themed windows. That lets `tre`'s M99 go ahead. The widgets Tesserae never wrapped (carousel, splitter, pagination, popover, segmented button, time input field) go to a new M45 after the gate. Alternative: build them all in M42.
- Q2 **Docking and the app shell.** Recommended: **M45, after Tesserae moves to 0.3.5 (M43)**, on the `dock_target`/`dock_drop` events and `set_active_panel`. Alternative: ask `tre` to backport those to a 0.3.4.x.
- Q3 **Search.**
  - Recommended: `search_bar` is MD3's: 56 px, a full pill, `surface_container_high`, a leading icon, a `text_input` with its placeholder, and trailing icon buttons.
  - `search_view` becomes `tesserae.overlays.SearchView`: MD3's docked results panel, anchored below the bar. It opens when the field has focus or text and closes on Escape or an outside press. Results are `(text, fn)` rows; the down arrow moves from the field into them; `on_query(fn)` hears typing.
  - Alternative: `tre`'s empty panel, filled and opened by the app.
- Q4 **The node graph.** Recommended: **`tre`'s viewport and nodes, plus what 0.3.4 now allows**: pan by dragging the background, zoom with the wheel about the pointer, drag nodes (pointer capture), and `edge(a, b)` curves (a `path`) that follow the nodes. Alternative: `tre`'s parity only (no dragging, no edges).

### Phase 1 — Search ✅
- Step 1: `search_bar`, from its fragment: a 56 px `surface_container_high` pill with elevation; a 24 px leading icon (`on_surface`, default `search`); a `text_input` whose `placeholder` is now hint text in `on_surface_variant` (tre's, and the fragment, put it in the field as its text), typed text in `on_surface` and a `primary` caret, labelled by the placeholder; 48 px trailing icon buttons (`on_surface_variant`). `.query` is a `Signal` of the text, and `.on_query(fn)` hears each change; parts `field`, `leading`, `trailing0`... `tesserae.overlays.SearchView`: MD3's docked results panel (`surface_container_high`, 28 px corners, elevation, `max_height`) of 56 px `body_large` rows, `menu`/`menuitem`. With a `bar`, it opens below it on the field's focus or typing when there are results; down from the field enters the rows, the arrows wrap, and up from the first returns to the field; a click or Enter calls the row and closes it; an outside press or Escape closes it. `set_results` attaches or detaches pre-built rows, closing when empty; `on_query` goes through the bar. `search_view` returns one (`bar=`, `results=`). `test_widgets_search.py` (tre-parity) is replaced by `tests/test_search.py` (9); the fragment tests read `.node`. Mutation-checked with 10 mutants: two survived at first (one break didn't apply and was redone; shrinking results wasn't tested) and each fails a test now. 1227 → 1233 passed. Docs: widget catalog, overlays guide, API index, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 2 — Date and Time ✅
- Step 1: `date_picker_day`, from its fragment: a 48 px target holding MD3's 40 px circle (the fragment is tre's 48 px circle) with a `body_large` number. Selected: `primary`/`on_primary`. Today, unselected: a 1 px `primary` outline, `primary` number. Outside the month: `on_surface_variant`. `.selected`/`.today`/`.outside_month` are `Signal`s; a click or Enter calls `on_click(day)`, leaving selection to the grid; `role="button"`, named by the day. `period_selector`, from its fragment, audited to MD3: 52×80, 40 px halves in a 1 px `outline` frame with 8 px corners (tre's was 52×72, 36 px, rounded, unframed); selected `tertiary_container`/`on_tertiary_container`, the other `on_surface_variant`. `.period` is a `Signal` with `.on_change`; a click or Enter selects, the arrows switch, and it's one Tab stop, the selected half. `time_input_field` (new, by Q1): 96×72 `surface_container_highest`, 8 px corners, a 45 px `display_medium` numeral in `on_surface`, centred by sizing the input to two measured digits (`text_input` takes neither `text_align` nor `line_height`) and re-centred after a re-colour. Focused: `primary_container`/`on_primary_container` with a 2 px `primary` outline. `unit` is hour (0–23) or minute (0–59). `.value` is a `Signal` shown as two digits; typing a number in range sets it and leaving puts the digits back; the arrows and `a11y_action` increment/decrement step and wrap; `.on_change`. The tre-parity tests for the day and period selector went; `tests/test_date_time.py` (13) replaces them, and the fragment tests now say how the fragment (tre's shape) and the widget (MD3's) differ. Mutation-checked with 10 mutants; one survived (a circular centring check after a re-theme) until the test pinned the width; now each fails a test. 1233 → 1242 passed. Docs: widget catalog — ✅

### Phase 3 — Media ✅
- Step 1: `image` and `video` are `Widget`s over a `kind: Image` spec, built with frames (new `Widget(frames=)`), so the compiler creates the node with `window.create("image")` and the pixels; they're off tre's `add_image_from_bytes`, `add_video` and `push_frame` (all on its removal list; `push_frame`'s replacement is `set(rgba=, pixel_width=, pixel_height=)`). `image` decodes the file with Pillow (still an `OSError` if it can't), checks `fit` (cover, contain, fill), and is decorative unless `label=` (then `role="img"`). `video` starts as a blank 1×1 frame; `frame(rgba, width, height)` checks the byte count, sets the pixels (frames can change size) and records them in the view's frames, so a re-colour, which re-applies them, keeps the latest. Found: the first `video` spec kept the id `image` while the widget was named `video`, so its frames were keyed under a name the view never read; a test re-colouring after a frame caught it. `tests/test_media.py` (6) replaces the image, video and icon parity tests. Mutation-checked with 6 mutants (including that id mix-up); each fails a test. 1242 → 1244 passed. Docs: widget catalog, component fragments, `api/spec.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `images.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 4 — The Node Graph ✅
- Step 1: Off tre's `add_node_graph`/`add_graph_node`, per Q4. Probed first: `scale` on a 0×0 box scales about its origin; hit testing follows the transform (a press lands on a scaled node where it's drawn, with `e.x` in its own units); `wheel` carries `delta_y` and the pointer. `node_graph`, from its fragment: a clipped `surface_container_low` viewport (`role="group"`) holding a 0×0 content box whose `translate_x`/`translate_y`/`scale` come from `.offset` and `.zoom` (`Signal`s). Dragging the background pans, with pointer capture, but not when the press is in a node; the wheel zooms by 1.1 per notch within 0.25–4, keeping the graph point under the pointer fixed. `graph.edge(a, b)`: a 2 px `outline` cubic `path` from `a`'s right-middle to `b`'s left-middle, in an edge layer under the nodes, re-routed when either moves and re-tinted on a re-colour. `graph_node`: a `surface_container_high` card (12 px corners, `outline_variant` border) with a 32 px `surface_container_highest` title bar (`title_small`) over its body, at graph coordinates; `.position` is a `Signal`; dragging moves it by the pointer's travel divided by the zoom, the arrow keys move it 8 px when focused, and `.on_move` hears the user's moves; `role="group"` named by its label. `tests/test_media.py` gains 8; the node-graph parity test is gone and the fragment test reads `.node`. Mutation-checked with 9 mutants: 8 caught, and the 9th showed the re-colour placement code was dead (a node's place isn't in its style), so it was removed. 1244 → 1251 passed. With this, no `tesserae.widgets` function delegates to tre. Docs: widget catalog, `ARCHITECTURE.md` Reviewed on 2026-09-26, after the move to Nobara: `node_graph.set_theme` didn't reach its nodes (the test re-themed each node by hand), and a graph node took keyboard focus with no ring and was clipped so one couldn't show. Fixed: the graph passes its theme on; the node gets MD3 feedback (a focus ring, the dragged state layer while dragged), and its title bar rounds its own top corners instead of the node clipping. Phase 2's period selector now puts its two `tab`s in a `tablist` (it was a `group`). Each fix is covered: undoing it fails a test. 1251 → 1252. — ✅

### Phase 5 — Segmented Button, Pagination, Popover ✅
- Step 1: new by Q1, checked against tre's dumps of `add_segmented_button`/`add_pagination`/`add_popover` (`legacy-widgets.md`, 0.3.5). `segmented_button` (`buttons.py`): equal segments in one 40 px pill with a 1 px `outline` border, padded 1 px so a selected fill never covers it, and 1 px `outline` dividers; the end segments and their feedback clips take per-corner radii (re-set after a re-colour, as a style holds one). Selected: `secondary_container`, an 18 px check (shown, and given its width and 8 px gap) before an `on_secondary_container` `label_large` label; otherwise `on_surface`. Without `width`, segments fit the widest measured label (12 px either side plus the check), rounded up to whole pixels. Single-select: `role="radio"` segments, a click selects (never deselects), one roving Tab stop, left/right select and focus, wrapping. `multi=True`: `role="checkbox"`, a click or Enter toggles, each a Tab stop, the arrows only move focus. `.selected` a `Signal` (index or `None`, or a `frozenset`), `.on_change` for the user's changes; `checked` announced. `pagination` (`navigation.py`): previous, a 40 px circle per page and next, 4 px apart; the current page `primary`/`on_primary`, `selected`; previous is `chevron_right` turned 180° (Tesserae's 12 icons have no `chevron_left`). At the ends previous/next are disabled: not focusable, `disabled`, `on_surface` at 38%, no feedback, clicks ignored. `.current` a `Signal`, `.on_change`; labels "Previous page", "Page N", "Next page". `tesserae.overlays.Popover` (and `widgets.popover`): `surface_container`, 12 px corners, elevation 2, padded 16; an optional `title_small` subhead and `body_medium` text in `on_surface_variant`, the text's height measured wrapped (`measure_text(max_width=)`) since the compiler measures one line; optional text-button actions (focus moves to the first; each calls its `fn` and closes). Not modal; stays until an outside press, Escape or an action; `attach(anchor)` toggles it on the anchor's click; `role="dialog"`. Tests: `tests/test_segmented_pagination.py` (16) and 5 in `test_overlays.py`. Mutation-checked with 16 mutants: 15 caught, and the 16th showed re-turning the arrow after a re-colour was dead (`rotation_deg` isn't a style property), so it was removed. 1252 → 1273 passed; all 3 examples clean. Docs: widget catalog (and its stale "thin delegates" sentence removed), overlays guide, API index, `ARCHITECTURE.md`; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 6 — Carousel and Splitter ✅
- Step 1: new by Q1, per `tre`'s `legacy-behavior.md` (0.3.5), in a new `widgets/containment.py`. `carousel`: a clipped, focusable `role="group"` box holding absolutely placed slots, each masked to 28 px corners (`extra_large`) on `surface_container_highest` and holding an item (a node or widget; `.add(item)`), 16 px in, 8 apart, 8 above and below. `multi_browse` (large/medium/small) and `hero` (large/small): `small` 56, `medium` 112, `large` what's left (at least 56), the pattern's last repeating, items before the current one small, the current one at the left padding. `.index` (a `Signal`) moves the fractional position there over 300 ms, standard easing, blending each item's `(x, width)` between the snapped layouts either side (`carousel_layout`, a pure function). `tre` animates no `width` and has no frame callback, so the position is the `stroke_width` of a private node in no tree, animated by `tre`, and `_Ticker` (a one-frame animation chained from `on_complete`) re-lays out each frame until it arrives. A wheel notch (down moves on), 60 px of drag from anywhere (with pointer capture; left moves on) and the arrows move it, clamped. `uncontained`: each item's own width (200 when it has none; an unsized node reads `"auto"`, found by a test), `.scroll` (a `Signal`) moved by half the wheel delta, 1:1 drag and the arrows, clamped to the content. `.on_change` hears the user's moves. `splitter(window, first, second, width, height, orientation, position)`: the two panes either side of a 16 px handle holding MD3's 4×48 `outline` drag handle, with `col_resize`/`row_resize`; the handle is a focusable `role="slider"` announcing its value. Dragging (pointer capture, MD3's dragged state layer) puts the handle's middle under the pointer, clamped to 0..1, at once; the arrows along its axis move it 5%, Home/End to the ends; `a11y_action` increment/decrement/set_value. `.position` a `Signal`, `.on_change` for the user's moves. `tests/test_carousel_splitter.py` (14). Mutation-checked with 17 mutants: 16 caught (two after tightening a test: the release checked over the handle, and a repeated Home), 1 equivalent (the rule "items before the current one are small" equals the pattern's last slot in both layouts). 1273 → 1287 passed; all 3 examples clean. Docs: widget catalog, API index (its stale "thin-delegate" line rewritten), `ARCHITECTURE.md`; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 7 — Off the Window's Theme ✅
- Step 1: `App` no longer calls `Window.set_theme`: `_set_window_theme`, `_window_theme` (M31's rewriting of the custom theme so the window matched the view's seed precedence) and `_OVERRIDDEN_SEED` are gone, and so is the re-assertion after an OS light/dark switch with a fixed choice (`tre` still flips its own window theme then, but nothing reads it). Nothing in `src/` sets or reads the window's theme; `tesserae.Theme` (`app.theme`, each view's) is the only theme. Tests: the M31 window-theme tests in `test_theme_reload.py` now check `app.theme` (seed precedence, a default theme's colours, no seed) plus a new `test_the_window_is_never_themed` (through `set_theme_specs` and `set_dark`); `test_dark_mode.py` checks `app.theme` and that the window stays unthemed; the live hot-reload test reads `app.theme`. The six fragment tests' `_themed_window` helpers (now `_window`) and `test_spec_expand`'s window no longer call `set_theme`, which nothing read. Still calling it on purpose: the `tre`-reference tests (`test_tokens`, `test_theme_object`, `test_spec_build`, `treediff`), which M43 retires. Mutation-checked: `App` theming its window again fails 5 tests. 1287 passed; all 3 examples clean. Docs: `api/app.md`, `api/spec.md`, `guide/themes-and-fonts.md` (the theme is the app's; its `Window.set_theme` example gone), `guide/hot-reload.md`, the design page, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, and the docstrings of `App`, `spec/themes.py` and the default theme; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 8 — Tests, Docs, Tracker, Gate ✅
- Step 1: The per-widget tests were written phase by phase, each mutation-checked (`test_search.py`, `test_date_time.py`, `test_media.py`, `test_segmented_pagination.py`, `test_carousel_splitter.py`, and additions to `test_overlays.py`). The gate: `tests/test_no_tre_controls.py` now also forbids M42's names, taken from `tre`'s own removal list (`python/tre/_removed.py` on its `0.3.5` branch, `279e640`): the search, date and time, media, node-graph, segmented-button, pagination, popover, carousel and splitter factories, `add_text_field`, `add_image_from_bytes`, `push_frame`, the carousel state methods and `build_shell`; and `set_theme`/`theme` on anything named a window (Tesserae's own `View`, `Widget` and controls have those names, so they count only there). A new test proves the scan sees them and not Tesserae's own; re-adding `self._window.set_theme(...)` to `App` fails it. `src/tesserae` uses none. Under the switch (that `_removed.py`, installed over the pinned 0.3.4 wheel from a pytest plugin and an example runner in the scratchpad; `hasattr(tre.Window, "set_theme")` and `hasattr(tre, "View")` confirmed `False`): all three examples run clean with no `RemovedError`; the suite is 739 passed / 419 failed, every failure a deliberate `tre`-reference test that M43 retires — `tre.View` 250 (binding parity, tokens, the theme object, spec build, tree parity, the error-parity checks in `test_view`, a clickable node "as in tre", `register` rejecting a `tre` view, the GC test for `tre` issue #10), `Window.set_theme` 154 (the same reference tests' themed windows) and `tre.Signal` 14 (`test_reactive`'s `tre` half), plus tree parity's corpus count, which shrinks when `tre`'s `View` can't load (against M41's 599/497 and M43's dry run of 327/583). Found under the switch and fixed, all in tests: Phase 7's new checks read `window.theme`, which 0.3.5 removes outright (now `helpers.window_unthemed`, which treats a missing `theme` as unthemed); `helpers.elevation` fell back to the removed `elevation` property for a node with no shadows (now level 0 when it's gone); and three fragment tests read the removed `border_width` (now `stroke_width`). Docs: the stale "thin delegate" descriptions of `tesserae.widgets` — its package docstring and six module docstrings, the five split-button fragments' notes, the architecture diagrams in `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `docs/architecture.md`, and the docs home — now say every widget is Tesserae's; `docs/architecture.md`'s "`View`/`Component` are still `tre`'s" is corrected; the design page records the gate result. 1287 → 1288 passed; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

## Milestone 43 — Migration Gate: Off `tre`'s Removed API

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 5 phases done (2026-09-26).** Tesserae's side of `tre` M97 Phase 2 Step 6: `tre` starts its M99 (removing the MD3 widgets, kinds and theming) only once Tesserae confirms, by running its suite and examples with `tre`'s `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED` switch on rather than on trust, that it uses nothing 0.3.5 removes. User: "Push it and scope M43", then "Push it and go with your recommendations for M43" (Q1–Q3 as recommended). Scoped from `tre`'s `0.3.5` branch (`279e640`: M97 at Step 6, M98 complete, M99–M103 not started, no 0.3.5 release yet) and M42 Phase 8's run under the switch.

**Where it stands.** `src/tesserae` and the three examples already pass the switch (M42 Phase 8), and `tests/test_no_tre_controls.py` keeps it that way. What fails under the switch is 419 tests that compare Tesserae against `tre` on purpose, as the reference while 0.3.4 has one:
- `tre.View` (250): `test_binding_parity` (every `{{ }}` expression evaluated by both), `test_tree_parity` + `treediff` (127 views built by both), `test_spec_build` (cascade and error messages), `test_view`'s error-parity checks, `test_tokens`/`test_theme_object` where they build a `View`, `test_clickable`'s "as in tre", `test_components_screens`' rejection of a `tre` view, and `test_thread_gc` (`tre` issue #10, a `tre` `View` bug).
- `Window.set_theme`/`Window.theme` (154): `test_tokens` and `test_theme_object`, comparing every role, shape, elevation and type token with `tre`'s theme.
- `tre.Signal` (14): `test_reactive`'s `tre` half (each behaviour test runs on both implementations).

Losing these would lose the proof that Tesserae matches `tre` (784 colour roles, 166 binding expressions, 127 trees), so the question is how to keep that proof once `tre` can't produce it.

**Decisions to settle:**
- Q1 **The reference tests.** Recommended: **record `tre`'s answers once, on 0.3.4, as data** (`tools/record_tre_reference.py` writing JSON under `tests/reference/`: each binding expression's value or error, each tree's node properties, every theme token, the error messages), and have the tests compare Tesserae with the recording, so the proof survives 0.3.5; the recorder stays, re-runnable on 0.3.4. The tests of `tre`'s own behaviour go: `test_reactive`'s `tre` half, `test_thread_gc` (a `tre` `View` bug) and the rejection of a `tre` view (no such thing in 0.3.5). Alternatives: skip them when `tre` lacks the name, and delete them at 0.3.5 (keeps nothing); or delete them now.
- Q2 **The switch in CI.** CI builds `tre` from the `v0.3.4` tag, which has no `_removed.py`. Recommended: **vendor a copy** as `tests/tre_removed.py`, its header naming the `tre` commit it came from (`279e640`), installed by `tests/conftest.py` when `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED=1` and by a small `tools/forbid_removed.py` runner for the examples; CI runs the suite and examples twice, plain and with the switch. Alternative: fetch the file from `tre`'s `0.3.5` branch in CI (always current, but CI then follows a moving branch).
- Q3 **Moving onto 0.3.5.** The switch proves Tesserae is ready; moving to 0.3.5 needs `tre` to finish M99–M103 and release it. Recommended: **M43 ends at the confirmation**, and the move becomes a new **M46 — Move to `tre` 0.3.5** (pin `.venv` and CI to the release, drop the switch and the vendored copy, retire what 0.3.5 still renames), with M45 (docking, which needs 0.3.5's API) after it. Alternative: keep M43 open until the release.

### Phase 1 — Dry Run ✅
- Step 1: a dry run (2026-09-25, at `tre`'s request for its M97 Phase 2 Step 6). `tre`'s `_removed.py` (0.3.5 branch, `c49b85f`) installed from a pytest plugin in the scratchpad, against the pinned v0.3.4 wheel. **`src/tesserae` uses nothing M98 removes** (static scan against every `REMOVED` table: no `View`, `Component`, reactivity, binding, `reconcile`, `instantiate`, `from_view`/`show_view`, `push_frame`, old property names or `Theme`). **What remains is M99's:** the 8 legacy MD3 kinds in `spec/build.py` with `get_checked`/`get_selected`/`set_on_change` (M40), ~45 factories in `tesserae.widgets` (M41–M42), and `Window.set_theme` in `App`/`View` (goes with them). Suite: 583 failed / 327 passed, nearly all at `Window.set_theme` (516), which every themed `App` and stand-alone `View` calls; the rest the factories and the deliberate reference tests. Examples: `counter` and `multi_screen` run clean with the switch on, `todo_list` stops at `add_checkbox`. Found and fixed an unused `from tre import View` in a test (`dae9ff3`). Reported to `tre` at the user's request — ✅

### Phase 2 — Decisions ✅
- Step 1: the user took all three recommendations: Q1, `tre`'s answers recorded once on 0.3.4 as data under `tests/reference/` by a re-runnable `tools/record_tre_reference.py`, the reference tests comparing with the recording, and the tests of `tre`'s own behaviour retired; Q2, a vendored `tests/tre_removed.py` (from `tre` `279e640`), installed when `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED=1`, with CI running plain and with the switch; Q3, M43 ends at the confirmation, and the move to 0.3.5 is the new M46, with M45 after it — ✅

### Phase 3 — Reference Tests Off `tre`'s Removed API ✅
- Step 1: `tests/reference.py`: a reference test wraps each question it asks `tre` in `reference.tre(fn)`, keyed by the test's id and the order it asks. Normally it returns the answer recorded for that test, or re-raises the recorded exception with its type (a builtin; else a stand-in) and message, without calling `fn`; with `TESSERAE_RECORD_TRE=1` it calls `fn` and records. A small tagged JSON form keeps tuples, non-string dict keys and exceptions exact; `reference.answer(key, fn, module)` covers questions asked outside a test (a recorded corpus). `tests/conftest.py` keys each test and saves a recording only if every test passed. `tools/record_tre_reference.py` runs the seven parity modules recording, refusing on a `tre` without `View`, `Signal` or `Window.set_theme` (checked under the switch: exit 2, nothing written). Recorded on the pinned 0.3.4: `tests/reference/*.json`, ~370 KB, 574 tests. Converted: `test_binding_parity` (the corpus too, so a `{{ }}` added after 0.3.5 isn't asked of `tre`), `test_tree_parity` + `treediff` (each case records the expanded spec, its frames in base64 and `treediff.tre_dump`, `tre`'s side as plain data; Tesserae builds the recorded spec, so later fragment edits don't need `tre`), `test_spec_build` (the cascade, fills, error messages; its `_window` no longer themes a window), `test_tokens` (every role for 8 seeds light and dark, overrides, seed precedence, shape, elevation, type and colour parsing), `test_theme_object`, `test_view`'s error parity and `test_clickable`'s "as in tre". Retired or reworked (Q1): `test_reactive`'s `tre` half (13 tests; the Tesserae half stays, and the exports test now checks the names are `tesserae.reactive`'s), `register()` rejecting a `tre` view (now any non-Tesserae object), and `test_thread_gc`, which now frees a `Window` and a node instead of a `View` (every 0.3.4 object is `ThreadBound`, so the guard still means something on 0.3.5). Every converted module collects exactly as many tests as before. New `tests/test_reference.py` (13): exact round trips, unrecordable objects, re-raised exceptions and a missing answer. **Mutation-checked with the switch on** (so no live `tre`): Tesserae's binding `==` across types, colours at `spec_version="2025"`, the cascade's id tier and the compiler's padding each fail their parity test against the recording; a tampered answer and a deleted one fail too. **Under `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED`: the whole suite passes** (1287, and the real-font test skips without `TRE_SOURCE_DIR`; M42 Phase 8 had 739 passed / 419 failed), and all three examples run clean. 1288 passed without it (−13 retired, +13 new). Docs: the design page's gate section, `installation.md` (re-recording), `ARCHITECTURE.md`; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 4 — The Switch in CI ✅
- Step 1: by Q2: `tests/tre_removed.py` is `tre`'s `python/tre/_removed.py` from its `0.3.5` branch at `279e640`, unchanged below a header naming its source and saying M46 deletes it. `tests/conftest.py` installs it before any test imports `tre` when `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED=1` (and stands aside for a `tre` that has its own switch, which installs itself on import); `tools/forbid_removed.py <script>` does the same for a script, run as `__main__` with its folder on `sys.path`. A new test, run only with the switch on, checks `tre.View`, `tre.Signal`, `Window.set_theme` and `Window.add_checkbox` really are gone, so a green switched run means something. Checked: a script calling `Window.set_theme` exits 1 with `RemovedError` under the runner and runs without it. `ci.yml` runs the suite and the three examples a second time with `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED: "1"` (its header comment's stale M42-era line replaced). Locally, CI's commands: plain 1288 passed (the switched-only test skipped); switched 1289 passed; all three examples clean both ways. The CI run itself happens when this is pushed. Docs: `installation.md` (running with the switch), the design page's gate section, `ARCHITECTURE.md`; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 5 — Confirm, Docs, Tracker ✅
- Step 1: pushed Phase 4 (`31fcc1b`); CI run 36293860118 green in every step, `tre` built from `v0.3.4` (`tre-0.3.4`): plain 1286 passed, 3 skipped (the two live `App.run` tests, no display on the runner, and the switched-only check); with `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED=1`, 1287 passed, 2 skipped; the three examples clean both ways. Reported to `tre`'s session (the peer "Milestone 5 (fork)", where M34's messages went) at the user's go-ahead: Tesserae uses nothing 0.3.5 removes or renames, proved with the switch; the commit, CI run and counts; what changed since the dry run (M40–M42's widgets, `App` off `Window.set_theme`, M43's recorded answers); next M46 then M45, and a request for a note or an updated `_removed.py` if M100's renames go beyond `279e640`'s list. **`tre` replied, having checked it against this repo** (`31fcc1b` on `main`, run 36293860118 green, `tests/tre_removed.py` identical to its `_removed.py` at `279e640` below the header): its M97 is complete (recorded in its tracker, `c348333`), M99 is unblocked with its start the user's call, and the request is its M100 Step 3 — an updated `_removed.py` or a note comes before the 0.3.5 release if the renames go further, so the gate can be rerun. **Rerun at `tre`'s request** (its M100 done, `0.3.5` at `07be406`): the new `_removed.py` adds only `Event.source` (use `event.target`) and `Event.position` (use `event.window_x`/`window_y`), confirmed by diffing it (read through the GitHub API) against the vendored copy; `tests/tre_removed.py` updated to it (header now `07be406`); both names checked to raise under the switch. Result: zero `RemovedError`s — 1289 passed with the switch, 1288 plain, all three examples clean; `src/tesserae` reads neither from an event (its only `.position` is its own widgets'). Reported back to `tre`. Docs: the design page records the confirmation; testing notes and `ARCHITECTURE.md` were written in Phases 3–4; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

## Milestone 44 — Logging with loguru

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-25).** User-directed: "Id like to use loguru for logs and console outputs for tesserae. Can we set that up while we wait for tre". Decisions (the user took all three recommendations): Tesserae's messages are **on by default**; a failed background hot reload is **logged, not raised through `tre`**; the **examples use `logger`**. Done while `tre` builds its M97 Phase 2 (M34 waits on it), outside the building-block program, so it's numbered after M43.

### Phase 1 — Logging ✅
- Step 1: `loguru>=0.7` added to `pyproject.toml`'s dependencies (0.7.3 installed in `.venv`; MIT, no dependencies of its own). CI installs it through `pip install -e .` — ✅
- Step 2: `tesserae/log.py`: Tesserae logs with loguru's shared `logger` and adds no handler, so the app's loguru setup decides where messages go (loguru's default stderr with none). `configure_logging(level="INFO", *, sink=sys.stderr, format=DEFAULT_FORMAT, capture_warnings=True)` replaces loguru's default handler (id 0) and leaves any handler the app added, and returns the handler id. With `capture_warnings` it shows Python warnings through loguru by replacing `warnings.showwarning`; `FontFallbackWarning` stays a real warning, so `filterwarnings("error", ...)` still raises. Exported as `tesserae.configure_logging` — ✅
- Step 3: what's logged. DEBUG: `app.load`, `app.show`, failed-reload tracebacks. INFO: "hot reload on: watching N screen(s) and M theme/stylesheet file(s)", each view reloaded (`ViewWatcher`, both `start` and `poll`), each theme or stylesheet file applied. ERROR: a background reload that failed, naming the file and the error (`hot reload of <file> failed: ...`), the view left as it was. A failure while reading or expanding files is logged on the watcher thread and nothing is queued; a `tre` rejection is logged when the loop runs the queued update. `poll()`, called from the app's own loop, still raises. Messages carry their module (`tesserae.spec.watch`, `tesserae.app`), so `logger.disable("tesserae")` or `logger.disable("tesserae.spec.watch")` silences them — ✅
- Step 4: the three examples use `configure_logging()` and `logger.info` instead of `print`; all run clean — ✅

### Phase 2 — Tests, Docs ✅
- Step 1: `tests/conftest.py` gains a `logs` fixture capturing loguru records (locked, since watcher threads log). The four background-failure tests now assert the logged ERROR, the DEBUG traceback, and that nothing was queued, instead of a raising callable; a new test covers a `tre` rejection logged on the loop. `tests/test_log.py` (6 tests): the format and module name, level filtering, `logger.disable("tesserae")`, warnings through loguru that still become errors under `filterwarnings("error")`, `capture_warnings=False`, and the "hot reload on" line. 419 → 426 passed — ✅
- Step 2: docs: new `guide/logging.md` (in the nav) — setup, what's logged at each level, failed reloads, warnings, turning messages off; `guide/hot-reload.md` and `api/spec.md` say failures are logged; `api/index.md` lists `configure_logging`; `README.md`; `ARCHITECTURE.md` gained a logging note and lost a stale M31-era line saying theme and stylesheet files weren't watched. `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅
## Milestone 45 — Docking and the App Shell

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 4 phases done (2026-09-27).** User: "Push it and go with your recommendations for M45" (Q1–Q4 as recommended). Moved out of M42 by its Q2 (user: as recommended); after M46. Started by the user's standing go-ahead ("start M46 and push it, then start on M45"), which covered pushing M46, not M45. `tre` 0.3.5 keeps docking as a bare mechanism (D10) and leaves its presentation and `build_shell` to the framework. Scoped from 0.3.5's stubs and `docs/guide/docking.md` (read at `v0.3.5`), 0.3.4's `build_shell`/docking source for what it did, and a probe on the 0.3.5 wheel.

**What 0.3.5 gives** (probed): `window.add_dock_zone(side, container, size)` registers a node the app builds as `left`/`right`/`top`/`bottom`/`center`'s zone; `dock_panel(side, panel)` docks a panel there and shows it; a zone shows one panel at a time by keeping only that panel as its child, and `set_active_panel(side, index)` swaps it; `start_panel_drag(panel)`, called from the framework's own handle's `pointer_down`, runs a drag whose `dock_target` window event reports the zone under the pointer (`event.side`, or `None`) and whose release docks the panel there and reports `dock_drop` (`event.panel`, `event.side`); a headless test drives it with `simulate`. `window.on` keeps one listener per event, so one docking manager must own `dock_target`/`dock_drop` for a window. **What 0.3.4 did that's gone:** `build_shell(menu_bar, toolbar, status_bar)` put those regions in a column around a `flex_grow` content container and returned it; `set_dock_handle`/`set_drop_zone_highlight` drew the handle and a highlight, with no tab strip for a zone's panels. MD3 has no docking component; its closest guidance is panes and tabs.

**Decisions to settle:**
- Q1 **The API.** Recommended: **a Python API first** -- `tesserae.docking.Dock(window)` (zones, panels with a title and optional icon, the drag, the highlight; it owns the window's docking events) and `tesserae.shell.AppShell(window, top_app_bar=, navigation=, status_bar=, dock=)` returning the content region; `App` gains `app.use_shell(shell)` so `show()` mounts screens in the shell's content instead of the window's root. A YAML form of the shell waits until the Python one has been used. Alternative: a declarative shell (a `*_Shell.yaml`) from the start.
- Q2 **How a zone looks and is used.** Recommended: **MD3's secondary tabs** as each zone's strip of panel titles (a click or Enter shows a panel; the arrows move between tabs), the **tab itself as the drag handle** (a press starts `start_panel_drag`), and a **`primary` highlight** over the target zone while dragging (12% fill, a 2 px outline, MD3's dragged state layer on the tab). Since `tre`'s drag is pointer-only, **each tab's context menu offers "Move to left/right/bottom"** (M41's `Menu.attach_context`, and Shift+F10 / the Menu key when focused), so keyboard and screen-reader users can move panels. Alternative: a plain drag handle bar per panel, pointer only.
- Q3 **Resizing zones.** Recommended: **yes, with M42's `splitter`** between each side zone and the content, the zone's `size` its starting share, so a user can widen the inspector. Alternative: fixed sizes (as `add_dock_zone`'s `size` gives), resizing later.
- Q4 **Remembering a layout.** Recommended: **`dock.layout()` / `dock.restore(layout)`** -- which panel is in which zone, which is shown, and the splitters' positions, as plain data an app can save -- but no automatic saving. Alternative: leave it out for now.

### Phase 1 — Decisions ✅
- Step 1: the user took all four recommendations: Q1, a Python API first -- `tesserae.docking.Dock` and `tesserae.shell.AppShell`, with `App.use_shell` -- the YAML shell later; Q2, MD3 secondary tabs per zone, the tab as the drag handle, a `primary` drop highlight, and a "Move to" context menu for keyboard and screen-reader users; Q3, splitters between side zones and the content; Q4, `dock.layout()`/`dock.restore(layout)` as plain data, no automatic saving — ✅

### Phase 2 — Docking ✅
- Step 1: probed first on the 0.3.5 wheel: a drop over no zone leaves the panel (`dock_drop` with `side=None`); `start_panel_drag` works from a `pointer_move`, so a click can stay a click; a zone keeps non-panel children; a drag updates both zones' lists; but **`dock_panel` on an already-docked panel leaves it listed in its old zone too** (a later `set_active_panel` there pulls it back, after which the panel is a child of both zones) -- filed at the user's request as [`tre` issue #14](https://github.com/mindderivative/tre/issues/14). `tesserae.docking.Dock(window, theme=)`: `add_zone(side, size)` returns a column -- a 48 px strip (`role="tablist"`, "Left panels"), a 1 px `surface_variant` divider, and the body registered with `add_dock_zone` -- `surface_container_low` (`surface` in the centre), `size` wide or tall, the centre filling. `add_panel(side, node, title)` docks it, shows it, gives an unlabelled panel `role="tabpanel"` named by its title, and rebuilds the strip: MD3 secondary tabs, 16 px either side of a measured `title_small` title, `on_surface`/`on_surface_variant`, a 2 px `primary` indicator under the shown tab, `role="tab"` with `selected`, one roving Tab stop, MD3 feedback (`Interaction`). A click or Enter shows a panel (`set_active_panel`); the arrows move along the strip, wrapping. A press that moves 4 px starts `start_panel_drag` with the dragged state layer on the tab; `dock_target` puts a `primary` 12% highlight with a 2 px outline over the zone's body; `dock_drop` removes it, clears the dragged state, and if the panel changed zone updates the model, rebuilds both strips, focuses the moved tab and calls `on_move(fn)`'s listeners. `move(panel, side)` runs the same drag with a synthetic release over the target (as `dock_panel` can't move a docked panel); a tab's context menu -- right click (at the pointer), the Menu key or Shift+F10 (below the tab) -- lists "Move to <side>" for the other zones (M41's `Menu`). `show`, `panels`, `shown`, `side_of`, `set_theme`. The `Dock` owns the window's `dock_target`/`dock_drop`. `tests/test_docking.py` (15), mutation-checked with 14 mutants: 12 caught at first, 2 after tightening tests (no drag threshold; the dragged state not cleared after a drop over no zone), so all 14. 1322 → 1337 passed. Docs: API index, `ARCHITECTURE.md`; the guide page comes with Phase 4; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

- Step 2: **`tre` 0.3.5.1** (released 2026-09-27, tag on `9cba06b`, 24 assets, issue #14 closed; checked on GitHub first) fixes `dock_panel` on a docked panel. Confirmed with the issue's repro on its wheel (the old zone's list shrinks; `set_active_panel("left", 1)` is out of range). `.venv` and CI now pin `v0.3.5.1`; `Dock.move()` is one `dock_panel` call plus the model update it and a drop now share (`_moved`), since `dock_panel` fires no `dock_drop`. A new test checks `move` sends no pointer event (it fails with the old simulated drag). The known gap moved to "Fixed gaps". Docs: `installation.md`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` — ✅

### Phase 3 — The App Shell ✅
- Step 1: `tesserae.shell.AppShell(window, top_bar=, navigation=, status_bar=, zones={side: size}, dock=, theme=)`: a vertical box at 100% of the window, so it follows resizes (checked with `window.resize`) -- the top bar; a middle row of the navigation, the left zone, a centre column (top zone, `content`, bottom zone) and the right zone; the status bar. Widgets given to it are detached from wherever they were and placed; zones come from a `Dock` (its own, or `dock=`). **Q3 adjusted, not changed in intent:** M42's `splitter` is a fixed-size two-pane widget sharing its width by proportion, but a shell's zones keep a pixel size while the content flexes as the window resizes, so each zone gets a resize handle with the splitter handle's look (a 16 px target, MD3's 4x48 `outline` grip, `col_resize`/`row_resize`, `role="slider"` announcing its size and bounds): a drag with pointer capture (MD3's dragged state), the arrows (16 px, a right or bottom zone growing back), Home and End; `size`/`set_size`, clamped between 120 px and 70% of the zone's area. `App.window` (new) and `App.use_shell(shell)`: `show()` mounts screens in `shell.content`; a screen already showing moves in; a shell on another window is refused. **Q4:** `shell.layout()` -- each zone's panel titles, shown title and size, as plain data -- and `restore(layout)`, which moves panels first, then shows and sizes, skipping zones or titles it hasn't (sizes are the shell's, so layouts live on the shell rather than `Dock`, which gains `titles`, `shown_title` and `panel(title)`). `tests/test_shell.py` (11): the frame's geometry, screens in the content, following a resize, dragging each kind of handle (4 cases, including shrinking), clamping and the keyboard, a layout round trip with unknown zones and titles, re-theming the shell, its dock and its widgets, and an unknown zone. Mutation-checked with 16 mutants; each fails a test. 1338 → 1349 passed; all 3 examples clean. Docs: `api/app.md` (`window`, `use_shell`, `show`), API index, `ARCHITECTURE.md`; the guide page comes with Phase 4; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 4 — Center Tabs, Example, Docs ✅
- Step 1: **`AppShell(..., center=True)`** (user: "start Phase 4 with center=True", after asking how the zones fit together): the middle is the dock's `center` zone, so `shell.content` is a tab strip over it, and `App.show` (now through `shell.show_screen`) docks each screen there as a tab titled by its name the first time and brings its tab forward after; screens stay alive side by side, can be dragged to an edge zone, and panels can join them. Without it, `content` shows one screen at a time as before. `layout()`/`restore()` include the center (size `None`). 3 tests (the center as tabs, moving screens and panels across with a layout round trip, `use_shell` docking a screen already showing); mutation-checked (the center ignored, a tab not brought forward, restore skipping the center: each fails a test). New **`examples/app_shell/`**: a top app bar, a navigation rail switching screens, a status bar, `center=True` with Home and Notes screens (a two-way text field) as tabs, Files/Outline/Properties/Console panels docked left, right and bottom; a panel moving zone updates the status bar and a `Computed` on Home; it checks itself (the rail and a screen's button open tabs, a move, a resize, the layout) and renders 20 frames; run in CI. New guide page **"App Shell & Docking"** (the shell, `center=True`, docking panels with the mouse and keyboard, saving a layout, what's not covered), in the nav and on the docs home; API index; `ARCHITECTURE.md`. 1349 → 1356 passed; all 4 examples clean; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

## Milestone 46 — Move to `tre` 0.3.5

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-27).** Split out of M43 by its Q3 (user: as recommended). Started on `tre`'s report of the release, by the user's standing go-ahead ("Once you receive the message from tre about the release, start M46 and push it, then start on M45"). M43 proves, with `tre`'s switch, that Tesserae uses nothing 0.3.5 removes; this moves onto the release once `tre` finishes its M99–M103. M45 (docking, on 0.3.5's API) follows it.

### Phase 1 — The Move ✅
- Step 1: `tre` v0.3.5 checked before acting, not taken on the message: a published, non-draft GitHub release (2026-09-27 07:40 UTC, tag `v0.3.5` on `main`'s merge commit `2e4ed35`, PR #13) with 24 assets. `.venv` now has the release's `cp314` manylinux wheel (fetched with `gh release download`, installed `--no-deps --force-reinstall`); `tre.View`, `tre.Signal` and `Window.set_theme` are gone from it. **On 0.3.5 with no switch: 9 failures, one cause** -- `helpers.elevation`'s fallback caught only the switch's `AttributeError`, and the release's `get("elevation")` raises `ValueError` (the fix `tre` named, M100's missed arms); catching both makes the suite green. Retired, as planned: the vendored `tests/tre_removed.py`, `tools/forbid_removed.py`, `conftest.py`'s switch and CI's second run; the switch-only test is now `test_tesserae_runs_on_a_tre_without_the_removed_api`, run always. CI checks out and builds `tre` at `v0.3.5`. The recorded reference answers stay; `tools/record_tre_reference.py` says re-recording needs a 0.3.4 environment. **The behaviour changes a name switch can't catch** (`tre`'s `docs/migrating-0.3.5.md`, read at `v0.3.5`), each checked against Tesserae's source: `on("click")` no longer makes a node focusable -- Tesserae always sets `focusable=True` itself; `set(text=...)` fires no `change` -- Tesserae already relied on that (M36); canvas, virtual lists, terminal scrolling and the clipboard -- unused; Ctrl+A in a text input -- a gain for the search bar, spin box and time field; `layout_x`/`layout_y` including transforms -- the one use (`interaction.py`, placing a ripple in a surface that `translate_x` moves, as a switch's or slider's does) becomes more correct; `window.resize()` sends no `resize` -- nothing listens for one. **Click bubbling:** `on("click")` bubbles to every ancestor's listener. A probe showed a nested YAML clickable firing both handlers -- but M39's test `test_a_press_on_a_nested_clickable_ripples_only_the_nearest` shows a *pointer* click already bubbled on 0.3.4 (it asserts both handlers fire), so 0.3.5 only makes keyboard and simulated clicks match. Making the innermost clickable take the click alone (`event.stop()` in every activation listener) was tried and reverted as a design change, not part of this move; it's listed as a known gap for the user to decide, the patch kept in the scratchpad. 1321 → 1322 passed (the switch-only test now runs); all three examples clean. Docs: `installation.md` and `README.md` (v0.3.5, a link to `tre`'s migration page, re-recording on 0.3.4), the design page, `ARCHITECTURE.md`; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

## Milestone 47 — Bindable Accessibility

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 4 phases done (2026-09-27).** User: "Scope the a11y bindable gap as M47", then "Push it and go with your recommendations for M47" (Q1–Q3 as recommended). Closes the known gap from M39 Phase 3: a YAML node's `a11y:` fields are fixed when the view is built, so a live status, a count in a label ("3 unread messages") or a panel hidden from screen readers while collapsed can't follow a `Signal`. Runs on 0.3.4 and is independent of `tre`'s release, so it needn't wait for M46.

**How it works today** (read from the source): `spec/build.py` checks `a11y:` (`_a11y_fields`: `label`, `role`, `hidden`, `live`, `level`, through `tesserae.a11y.check`) and sets it once, at build and on each patch (`_a11y_props`, which also makes a clickable node a focusable `button` and resets dropped fields). Bindings are separate: `bindings: {prop: "{{ expr }}"}`, evaluated with dependency tracking by `View._wire_binding` and applied by `view._apply`, which knows text, sizes, colours, elevation and the controls' state -- nothing accessible. The macro layer substitutes only a bare `{{ name }}` in fragments, so `{{ status.get() }}` passes through expansion untouched, as `bindings:` already do. `tre` 0.3.4 can't report what a screen reader announces, so tests check the node's properties (`label`, `a11y_hidden`, `level`), not the speech.

**Decisions to settle:**
- Q1 **Where a binding is written.** Recommended: **inside `a11y:` itself** -- `a11y: {label: "{{ unread_summary.get() }}", live: polite}` (a whole-value binding, as `bindings:` take; corrected in Phase 2 from an example that mixed text in, which the binding language doesn't allow) -- so an accessible field reads the same bound or not, in the one expression language views already use; a value that contains `{{ }}` is a binding, anything else stays fixed and checked at build as now. Alternative: dotted keys in `bindings:` (`bindings: {"a11y.label": "{{ ... }}"}`), which keeps every binding in one place but splits a node's accessibility across two fields.
- Q2 **Which fields can be bound.** Recommended: **`label`, `hidden` and `level`** -- values that change as the app runs. `role` and `live` stay fixed and a binding on them is an error naming the field: a role decides what the node is (and, for a clickable node, its focus and `button` role), and `live` is how changes are announced, a design choice rather than state. Alternative: all five.
- Q3 **Python widgets.** `tesserae.widgets`' factories and `tesserae.controls` take `label=` as a string. Recommended: **also add `tesserae.a11y.bind(node, label=..., hidden=..., level=...)`**, taking a `Signal`, `Computed` or zero-argument function per field and returning the function that stops it, so an imperative widget's label can follow state as a YAML one can. Alternative: YAML only.

### Phase 1 — Decisions ✅
- Step 1: the user took all three recommendations: Q1, a binding is written inside `a11y:` (a value containing `{{ }}`); Q2, `label`, `hidden` and `level` can be bound, and a binding on `role` or `live` is an error naming the field; Q3, `tesserae.a11y.bind(node, label=, hidden=, level=)` for Python widgets, taking a `Signal`, `Computed` or zero-argument function per field and returning the function that stops it — ✅

### Phase 2 — Bound `a11y:` in Views ✅
- Step 1: `spec/build.py`: `is_binding`/`a11y_bindings`/`A11Y_BINDABLE` (`label` → `label`, `hidden` → `a11y_hidden`, `level` → `level`); `_a11y_fields` checks only the fixed fields and rejects a bound `role` or `live` ("a11y role can't be bound -- only label, hidden, level can follow a binding"); a patch leaves bound fields alone rather than resetting them. `View._wire_a11y` (wired with `bindings:`, undone and redone on every reconcile, re-theme and re-style): dependency-tracked evaluation, `_a11y_value` checks it with `tesserae.a11y.check` (a label a string, `hidden` a bool, `level` a whole number from 1; the evaluator's `None` handle clears a label or level; any other object is an error), set only when changed, on the node that carries accessibility (a Link's box, a TextField's `text_input`, a control's target), errors naming the widget, field and binding. **Found and fixed:** a binding must be the whole value (the binding language has no interpolation, so "`{{ unread.get() }} unread`" is "not wrapped"; the docs say to build the string in a `Computed`); a Link's text binding set its label on every change, overriding a fixed `a11y:` label (now only a Link that `a11y:` doesn't name); and **a control's `a11y:` fields were checked and then dropped** (a YAML Checkbox with `a11y: {label: "Agree"}` had no label, since M40): they now reach its node at build and on patch. A binding in a fragment passes through expansion while the fragment's params substitute. `tests/test_a11y_bindings.py` (18): the three fields following a `Computed`/`Signal`s beside fixed ones, surviving a re-theme, re-style and patching reconcile, a reconcile turning a binding fixed and back, `None`, five error cases, bound `role`/`live`, mixed text, a TextField, a Link's bound and fixed labels against its bound text, a control's fixed label (build, re-theme, patch) and bound label, and a fragment. Mutation-checked with 11 mutants: 10 caught (the control's patch after a test gained a patching reconcile), 1 equivalent (a patch resetting a bound field, which the rewire that follows every patch sets again). 1288 → 1308 passed (18 tests + the new docs block's 2 paint checks); 1307+ with the switch on. Docs: interaction guide ("Bound fields"; its "Not yet covered" bullet now points to Phase 3), bindings guide, `ARCHITECTURE.md`; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 3 — Python: `tesserae.a11y.bind` ✅
- Step 1: `tesserae.a11y.bind(node, label=, hidden=, level=)` (and `a11y.BINDABLE`): each field a `Signal`/`Computed` (anything with `.get()`), a function of no arguments, or a plain value; one `Effect` per field, so each follows only what it reads; each value checked by `check` (errors say "a11y.bind: ..." and name the field) and set only when it changed; takes a node, or a widget or control (its `.node`); returns the stopper. `role`, `live` and unknown fields are refused ("set role and live with describe"); if a field fails while binding, the ones already set up are disposed; a bad value later raises from the `Signal.set` that caused it. 9 tests in `tests/test_a11y_bindings.py` (27 in all): the three sources on a button, a node with a plain value and `stop()`, a checkbox's bound label surviving its own repaint and a re-theme, four refusals, the undo on a failed bind, and a later bad value. Mutation-checked with 7 mutants; each fails a test. 1308 → 1317 passed; 1318 with the switch on. Docs: interaction guide (`bind` beside `describe`; its "Not yet covered" bullet gone), API index, `ARCHITECTURE.md`; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 4 — Tests, Docs, Tracker ✅
- Step 1: `examples/counter`: the count text is a polite live region (`a11y: {live: polite}`), and the button's name is bound, `a11y: {label: "{{ button_label.get() }}"}`, a `Computed` of "Increment, count is N" (the ViewModel now keeps `count` as a `Signal` and derives both strings); `app.py` asserts the name after its three clicks, and the keyboard-only test in `test_a11y.py` checks the live region and that the name follows Enter and Space. The example runs clean plain and with `TRE_FORBID_REMOVED=1`. The docs were written with Phases 2–3 (interaction guide, bindings guide, API index, `ARCHITECTURE.md`); this pass adds a line to the docs home. The known gap moved to "Fixed gaps". 1317 passed, 1318 with the switch; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

## Milestone 48 — Hot Reload for `register()`ed Screens

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 3 phases done (2026-09-27).** User: "Scope hot reload for register()ed screens as M48", then "Push it and go with your recommendations for M48" (Q1–Q2 as recommended). Closes the known gap from M29 Phase 6: `App.run(hot_reload=True)` watches only screens `App.load()` made, so a screen given to `App.register()` -- the path for a `ViewModel` that needs the `app` (`examples/multi_screen/`) -- never reloads. Independent of `tre`'s release.

**What the source says** (`app.py`, `view.py`, `spec/watch.py`): `_start_watchers` starts a `ViewWatcher(view, path)` for each registered screen whose `_Registered.path` is set, and only `load()` sets it. But the screen already knows its file: a `View` built from a path keeps it as `view.path` (`view.py`, set before `build_view_spec`), `App.build_view()` builds from a path, and `register()`'s `view.move_to(window)` keeps `path` while rebuilding in the app's window. So the gap's own suggestion -- a `path=` on `register()` -- isn't needed: the watcher can take the path from the view. A view built from a spec dict has no file and can't be watched. `ViewWatcher` already reconciles in place (the ViewModel, its `app` reference and its bindings survive, rewired), so a registered screen reloads exactly as a loaded one does. The "hot reload on: watching N screen(s)" line counts watched screens.

**Decisions to settle:**
- Q1 **How a registered screen's file is found.** Recommended: **from the view** (`view.path`), so every file-built screen -- `register()`ed or `load()`ed -- is watched with no API change, and `_Registered.path` goes. Alternative: an explicit `register(..., path=)`, as the gap first proposed; clearer at the call site, but a second, redundant way to say what the view already knows.
- Q2 **A registered screen with no file** (built from a dict). Recommended: **say so once** when hot reload starts -- an INFO line naming the screen ("not watched: built from a spec, not a file") -- so a missing reload isn't a mystery. Alternative: skip it silently, as today.

**Out of scope, named:** components added at run time with `tesserae.instantiate` (a `Repeater`'s rows, say) are built from their own files; whether `ViewWatcher` follows those is a separate question, to check (not assumed) in Phase 2 and list as a known gap if it doesn't.

### Phase 1 — Decisions ✅
- Step 1: the user took both recommendations: Q1, a registered screen's file comes from its view (`view.path`), with no API change, and `_Registered.path` goes; Q2, a registered screen with no file is named once, at INFO, when hot reload starts — ✅

### Phase 2 — Watch Every File-Built Screen ✅
- Step 1: `App._start_watchers` takes each registered screen's file from its view (`view.path`), so a screen built with `build_view()` -- or `tesserae.View(path)` on its own, which `register()` moves into the app's window keeping `path` -- and given to `register()` is watched as a `load()`ed one is; `_Registered.path` (set only by `load()`) is gone. A screen with no file logs "hot reload: screen 'X' isn't watched -- it was built from a spec, not a file" once, at INFO; the "hot reload on: watching N screen(s)" count includes registered screens. `run()`'s docstring updated. `tests/test_register_reload.py` (3): a `register()`ed screen reloads in place on an edit (same node, the live bound value kept, the same ViewModel and its `app` reference, still wired); a `tesserae.View(path)` registered is watched; a `load()`ed and a `register()`ed screen are both counted and a spec-built one is named. `test_hot_reload_live.py`'s real `App.run(hot_reload=True)` now runs for `load` and `register` (both pass on a display). Mutation-checked: the old load-only rule fails 5 tests (including the live `register` run), dropping the log line fails 1. **Checked, not assumed:** a component added at run time with `tesserae.instantiate` isn't watched (a probe edited its `*_View.yaml`; `poll()` saw nothing and the running component kept its old width) -- listed as a known gap and in the hot-reload guide's limits. 1317 → 1321 passed; 1322 with the switch; examples clean. Docs: `guide/hot-reload.md`, `api/app.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 3 — Docs, Example, Tracker ✅
- Step 1: the hot-reload guide, `api/app.md`'s `run()`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `run()`'s docstring were updated in Phase 2; this pass adds `guide/apps-and-screens.md` (a registered screen is hot-reloaded), `api/app.md`'s and `register()`'s own docstring (a file-built view keeps its file), and `examples/multi_screen/app.py`'s notes. The known gap moved to "Fixed gaps"; the new one (runtime components) stays open. 1321 passed; `mkdocs build --strict` clean; the example runs clean — ✅

## Milestone 49 — A Click Goes to the Innermost Clickable Only

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 3 phases done (2026-09-27).** User: "M49 start with recommendations", then "Start phase 3 with recommendations" (Q1–Q4 as recommended), after "Scope all as separate Milestones, 2 will be M49 as previously discussed" (the four named candidates, with nested clicks as M49). This closes the known gap from M46. Since `tre` 0.3.5, a `click` bubbles to every ancestor's listener. A pointer click already did from M37, as M39's test pins, and since 0.3.5 keyboard and simulated clicks do too. So a clickable inside a clickable (a YAML `on_click` Rect in another, a button in a clickable card, a checkbox in a list row) fires both handlers. MD3 gives the click to the innermost only, as do Compose and Flutter. This is a behaviour change, so the user decides it.

**What the source says:** `tre`'s `Event.stop()` "ends propagation: no listener on a further ancestor runs". Every event bubbles except `pointer_enter`, `pointer_leave` and `change`. Tesserae adds its activation listeners in seven places:
- YAML `on_click` (`View._wire_handler`, through `_EVENTS`)
- `Widget.on_click` (`widgets/_composed.py`)
- `Control._on_click` and the `SpinBox` buttons (`controls.py`)
- the `Menu` items, the `Popover` anchor and the `NavigationDrawer` rows (`overlays.py`)
- the `Dock` tabs, for `click` and `secondary_click` (`docking.py`)

`Interaction` also listens for `click`, for feedback only, and it already knows the nearest interactive node (`_mine`, `_INTERACTIVE`), so a press ripples only the innermost node. All listeners for a node and event share one `Listeners` dispatcher, so a `stop()` from one of them doesn't starve the node's other listeners, only its ancestors. M46 tried a `handled(fn)` wrapper that calls `event.stop()` after an activation handler; it was reverted as a design change, and the patch is kept.

**Decisions to settle:**
- Q1 **How the click stops.** Recommended: **the innermost handler takes it.** Each activation listener Tesserae adds calls `event.stop()` after it runs, and nothing further up hears the click. This is `tre`'s own mechanism, it keys on handlers and not on feedback, and it's the same rule for YAML, Python widgets and controls. Alternative: filter by nearest clickable, as the ripple does: ancestors' handlers skip a click whose target sits under a nearer clickable. That leaves the event bubbling for any raw `node.on` listener, but it defines "clickable" by feedback (`interaction: true` with no handler would swallow clicks).
- Q2 **A disabled control inside a clickable.** Recommended: **it still takes the click, and nothing fires.** Tapping a disabled button in a card shouldn't open the card. Android views behave this way. Alternative: a disabled control lets the click through to its ancestor.
- Q3 **`secondary_click` too.** Recommended: **yes, by the same rule**, so a right click on a `Dock` tab inside a clickable region opens only the tab's menu. Alternative: `click` only.
- Q4 **A way for an ancestor to hear it anyway** (analytics, "any click in this panel"). Recommended: **not now; name it.** Nothing in the repo needs it, and a `stop()` is easy to relax later with an opt-out. Alternative: a YAML `click_through: true` on the inner node.

### Phase 1 — Decisions ✅
- Step 1: the user took all four recommendations. Q1: the innermost handler takes the click, since each activation listener Tesserae adds calls `event.stop()`. Q2: a disabled control still takes the click. Q3: `secondary_click` follows the same rule. Q4: there's no way for an ancestor to hear it anyway, and that's named as later work — ✅

### Phase 2 — Innermost Clicks ✅
- Step 1: `tesserae.listeners.handled(fn)` calls `fn(event)`, then `event.stop()`, even if `fn` raises. It wraps all 10 activation listeners Tesserae adds: YAML `on_click` (`View._wire_handler`, for `click` only), `Widget.on_click`, `Control`'s click (so a disabled control stops it too, by Q2), the `SpinBox` buttons, `Menu` items, `Menu.attach_context` (`secondary_click`), the `Popover` anchor, `SearchView` rows, and the `Dock` tabs' click and `secondary_click`. `Interaction`'s own `click` listener is feedback and isn't wrapped. M39's nested-press test now expects one click, not two. `tests/test_innermost_click.py` (9): a pointer click reaches the inner Rect alone, and the outer one when clicked outside it; keyboard Enter and a simulated click behave the same; a Text or Rect with no handler inside a clickable still passes the click up; a checkbox in a clickable card toggles without opening the card, and when disabled does nothing and still doesn't open it; `Widget.on_click`, the `SpinBox` and menu items take their clicks (a menu is on an overlay layer, so the test listens on its surface, not the root); a right click opens only the innermost context menu; the `Popover` anchor and a search result take their clicks; the `Dock` tabs take clicks and right clicks; and `handled` stops even when the handler raises. Mutation-checked: removing the wrapper at each of the 10 sites, and each of `handled`'s two guarantees, fails a test (12/12 caught). The mutation script runs without bytecode caching, because two same-size mutants of one file written within a second had reused a stale `.pyc` and faked a catch. 1356 → 1365 (1364 passed, 1 skipped); all four examples clean. Docs: the interaction guide (only the innermost one responds, including labels, disabled controls, keyboard and right clicks, and the change since M39) and `ARCHITECTURE.md` (`handled`); `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 3 — Docs, Examples, Tracker ✅
- Step 1: the interaction guide and `ARCHITECTURE.md` were updated in Phase 2. This pass adds the controls guide (a control in a clickable row or card takes its own click, even when disabled) and the widget catalog (an actionable card's `on_click` runs only for a click elsewhere on it). No example nests clickables, and all four run clean. The known gap moved to "Fixed gaps". 1364 passed, 1 skipped; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

## Milestone 50 — `tesserae.widgets` Follow the App's Theme

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 4 phases done (2026-09-27).** User: "Start 50 with recommendations", then "Start phase 3" and "Start phase 4" (Q1–Q3 as recommended), after "Scope all as separate Milestones" (the four named candidates). This closes the known gap from M40 Phase 5. The stateful controls and composed widgets in `tesserae.widgets` take `theme=` (MD3's baseline without one) and don't hear `App.set_dark`, the OS switching light and dark, or `App.set_theme_specs`. YAML controls in a view do. M45's shell and dock don't either.

**What the source says:**
- `App` keeps a theme's inputs and resolves a fresh `Theme` on each `app.theme`. `Theme` is a frozen dataclass, so following means swapping the object.
- `set_dark`, the `color_scheme` event and `set_theme_specs` re-colour only `App._built` (views from `build_view`), through `_apply_all`, which rolls back if any view fails. The list is strong and never pruned, and it skips the shell (`use_shell`).
- Every widget factory's result already has `set_theme`: the `Widget`s, `Control`/`SpinBox`/`Indicator`, and the overlays, which pass it to their widget. So do `Dock` and `AppShell`. `image` and `video` take no `theme=` at all.
- Nothing links a widget to an app. There's no global or per-window registry, and `tre`'s `Window` and `Node` can't be weak-referenced. The Python wrappers (`Widget`, `Control`) can be.
- Tests pin that a widget with no `theme=` on a bare window uses the baseline (`test_composed_widgets.py`, `test_fragments_selection.py`).
- `examples/app_shell/` passes `theme=app.theme` everywhere and builds its panels with raw `View(..., theme_seed=)`, so they don't follow either.

**Decisions to settle:**
- Q1 **How a widget finds its app.** Recommended: **implicitly, by its window.** An `App` registers its window, keyed by `id()` and cleared when the app closes, and a factory given no `theme=` on an app's window takes the app's theme and follows it. On a bare window, a widget keeps today's baseline, so those tests still hold. Apps need no code changes, and the example drops its `theme=` arguments. Alternative: explicit `app.follow(widget)` / `app.unfollow(widget)`. It's clearer, but opt-in and easy to forget.
- Q2 **An explicit `theme=`.** Recommended: **it pins the widget.** A widget given a theme keeps it and doesn't follow, so an app can have a fixed-colour region. Alternative: `theme=` is only the starting theme, and the widget still follows.
- Q3 **What else follows.** Recommended: **the shell and its dock** (`use_shell`), **overlays**, and **views made with `tesserae.View(..., window=app.window)`**, besides `build_view`'s. All of them go through the same rollback walk. Followers are held weakly (a `WeakSet` of wrappers), and a destroyed widget leaves at once. Alternative: widgets and controls only.

### Phase 1 — Decisions ✅
- Step 1: the user took all three recommendations. Q1: a widget finds its app by its window, and one made with no `theme=` on an app's window takes the app's theme and follows it, while a bare window keeps the baseline. Q2: an explicit `theme=` pins a widget. Q3: the shell and its dock, overlays, and views on the app's window follow too — ✅

### Phase 2 — Widgets and Controls Follow ✅
- Step 1: `tesserae.follow`: `App.__init__` calls `register_app`, which keeps a weak reference keyed by `id(window)` (`tre`'s `Window` can't be weakly referenced, and a live app keeps its window alive, so the id can't be reused). `initial_theme(window, theme, owner)` pins a given theme (Q2); with none, on an app's window, it returns `app.theme` and adds the owner to `app._followers`, and on a bare window it returns the baseline. `Widget`, `Control`, `SpinBox` and `Indicator` start from it, and their `destroy`/`dispose` call `unfollow`. **Held strongly, not weakly** (changed from the scoping's `WeakSet`): a widget no one kept a variable for, such as a divider, would otherwise stop following while its nodes stayed on screen. So a follower whose node was destroyed under it is dropped at the next re-theme (`alive`). Followers are an insertion-ordered dict, so a failed re-theme's rollback is predictable. `App._retheme` re-colours the `build_view` views, then the followers, as one `_apply_all` with rollback, used by `set_dark`, the `color_scheme` event and `set_theme_specs`. A seedless app's theme has no roles, so its widgets keep the baseline as before and no existing test changed. `tests/test_theme_following.py` (10): a button follows `set_dark` both ways; a checkbox, spin box and progress bar follow; the OS switch and `set_theme_specs` reach followers; an explicit theme pins; a bare window keeps the baseline; each kind stops following on `destroy`; a node destroyed under its widget is dropped; a widget no one kept still follows after `gc.collect()`; a failing follower rolls back the other widgets and the app's `dark`; each app is found by its own window. Mutation-checked, 14/15 caught; the survivor, `app_of`'s `app.window is window` guard, was dead code (the id can't be reused while the app lives) and was removed. 1364 → 1374 passed; all four examples clean. Docs: the controls guide, widget catalog, themes guide and `api/app.md` (the widgets follow, `theme=` pins, and the snippets no longer pass `theme=app.theme`, which would now pin), and `ARCHITECTURE.md` (`follow.py`); `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 3 — Shell, Dock, Overlays, Views ✅
- Step 1: a `tesserae.View` on an app's window with no theme argument at all takes `app._view_theme()` and follows. `dark` now defaults to `None`, so `dark=False` pins, as a seed or theme spec does, and a view on its own window is unchanged. `Dock` and `AppShell` start from `initial_theme`. Followers may define `_follow_theme(theme, view_theme)`, which `follow.retheme` prefers to `set_theme`: a view takes the app's theme arguments, and a following shell re-colours itself and **the dock it made only**, not the bars, rail or dock it was given. That keeps a pinned bar pinned, which the scoping hadn't foreseen: `shell.set_theme` re-themes its parts, and following through it would override their pins. A view answers `_follow_alive` itself, since its `node` is a lookup method. Overlays follow through their widgets, including `NavigationDrawer`'s scrim and drawer. `examples/app_shell/` drops every `theme=` and its panels' `theme_seed=`/`dark=`, and checks that `app.set_dark(True)` re-colours the shell, bar, dock zones and a panel's text. `tests/test_theme_following.py` +7 (17): a following view, and a seed, `dark=` alone or both pin; a view on its own window is unchanged; a view whose root was destroyed is dropped; the shell and its dock follow while a pinned bar doesn't and the rail follows by itself; a shell leaves a pinned dock it was given alone and a lone dock follows; a dialog and a `NavigationDrawer` follow; a failing follower rolls back a following view and shell. Mutation-checked, 12/12 caught (the first run's survivor, `dark=` alone pinning, got its own assertion). 1374 → 1381 passed; all four examples clean. Docs: the overlays and app-shell guides (no `theme=`, what follows and what pins) and `ARCHITECTURE.md`; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅

### Phase 4 — Docs, Tracker ✅
- Step 1: the controls, widget-catalog, overlays, themes and app-shell guides, `api/app.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` were updated in Phases 2 and 3. This pass adds the widget catalog's opening note (what follows, what pins, and a bare window's baseline) and the themes guide's light and dark section (the widgets, controls, overlays, shell and views switch with the screens). It also removes the controls guide's "not yet covered" note that Python controls don't follow. No page still passes `theme=app.theme`. The known gap moved to "Fixed gaps". 1381 passed, 1 skipped; all four examples clean; `mkdocs build --strict` clean — ✅
